High-quality new build office building with 2 commercial 
units at GF. The design & materiality reflect a 
contemporary interpretation of the rich architectural 
context of the St James’s Conservation Area.

01061 - Jermyn Street Piccadilly – Detailed Architectural Specification - Detailed Revisions
Client: GPE

A10
Project Particulars
Clauses
110 Project Particulars 
1. The Project is located at 180 Piccadilly and 48-50 Jermyn Street, W1J 9HF, London.
2. The Project - 180 Piccadilly - is a high-quality new-build office building with two commercial units at 

ground floor.  The design and materiality reflect a contemporary interpretation of the rich 
architectural context of the St James’s Conservation Area.  The building is a corner plot, fronting 
Piccadilly, Jermyn Street and Duke Street St James’s and the façade design is subtly different on 
each façade to respond to the different character of each street.  The western elevation is blank, 
adjoining the neighbouring buildings. 
  
The building has a single basement level, making use of existing vaults beneath the pavements, 
with ground floor and seven office floors above.  The upper three floors have south facing terraces 
and the upper two floors are within a mansard roof with dormer windows.  There is a communal 
roof terrace surrounding an external plant enclosure at the top. 

120 Two Stage Procurement of JCT Design & Build Contract 
1. The Main Contract Works are being procured through a Two Stage Design & Build process 

comprising: 
1.1. Pre Contract: (PCSA) procurement is based on the Design requirements included in the 

PCSA Tender information. During the First D&B Stage, certain packages will be jointly 
procured by the preferred Main Contractor and Client Team to inform the agreement of the 
Contract Sum.

1.2. Post Contract: (JCT D&B construction contract). Contract will be awarded, incorporating the 
Contract information and the Design, at the end of the second D&B Stage, with novation of 
the Design Team as depicted in the Employer's Requirements, and set out in the Design 
Responsibility Matrix.

2. The Contractor shall examine the Design, accept full responsibility for its entirety and complete the 
Detailed Design, etc. and deliver the Works, such that they maintain or exceed the quality, 
performance criteria and design/ visual intent of the Design.

3. The Design will generally be of a descriptive nature, requiring the appointment of suitable 
specialist subcontractors, as necessary, to complete the Detailed Design. Refer to Clause A21.140 
herein for further details.

4. Specialist subcontractors are to complete the Detailed Design, manufacture, supply and 
installation of fully warranted and guaranteed systems, etc. as described in the Specification. The 
Detailed Design submissions are to be reviewed for performance and compliance within the 
Design by the Employer’s Agent and/or the novated Design Team. Any proposed alternatives are 
to be fully visually compliant and be equal or better, in terms of robustness, performance, 
guarantee, suitability and quality to that specified or implied.

5. The Design may include a limited number of Prescriptive Elements, which are for incorporation in 
the Works, strictly in accordance with the Employer's Requirements and manufacturer's 
recommendations.

Ω End of Section

Make Limited A10

01061 - Jermyn Street Piccadilly – Detailed Architectural Specification - Detailed Revisions
Client: GPE

A21
Definitions and Compliance
Clauses
110 Definitions 
1. Defined Terms: The following definitions appear from time to time throughout the Specification. 

Where reference is made using slightly different terms they are to be interpreted as one of the 
below by the Architect. If unsure, request clarification at time of Tender.

2. Design Stages: Design to be carried out as follows: 
2.1. Prior to PCSA tender: Design requirements produced prior to award of the PCSA.
2.2. Prior to Contract Award: Completion of the Design for inclusion in the Employer's 

Requirements, and any Detailed Design carried out under the PCSA by the Contractor. 
(Note: Detailed Design is carried out both pre-contract and post-contract by the Design 
Team or the Contractor).

2.3. Post-Contract: Completion of the Detailed Design (including Technical Proposals and 
Working Drawings) by the Contractor or specialist subcontractors after Contract award.

3. Employer's Agent: The company or person representing the Client which may also be referred to 
as Employer's Representative or Employer herein.

4. Specification/ Architectural Specification/ this specification: This Specification, comprising 
sections A to Z inclusive, representing the architectural design requirements, which links the 
products, systems and materials shown on the Design Drawings to the Specification via a Material 
and System Sheet (MSS).

5. Design: All information (drawings, schedules, specifications and other supplemental information) 
forming part of the Employer’s Requirements (the Contract information and the Contractor’s 
Proposals) and agreed for Contract purposes, which sets out Descriptive Design, the visual intent, 
any Prescriptive Elements, performance criteria and minimum quality standards to be maintained 
in the completion of the Detailed Design by the Contractor.

6. D(d)etailed D(d)esign: Working Drawings, schedules, detailed specifications and Technical 
Proposals to complete the Design and confirm the final agreed solution to meet the fundamental 
intrinsic requirements of the Design completed by, or on behalf of the Contractor.

7. Working Drawings: Construction drawings and supporting information produced to an appropriate 
scale, showing final co-ordinated layouts, configuration, details, interfaces and finishes of the 
Works.

8. Technical Proposals: Detailed specifications, calculations, assembly drawings, technical 
submittals, data sheets, etc. provided by a specialist subcontractor confirming the final agreed 
solution to the Design.

9. Descriptive Design: Design information, performance criteria and Visual Reference that describe 
the visual intent of the Works, parts of the Works or elements within the Works where the specialist 
subcontractors shall be responsible for meeting the visual design intent and the requirements of 
the Specification, including any Visual References, including the performance criteria.

10. Visual Reference: Where the Design is of a descriptive nature, proprietary products or materials 
may be identified to indicate the visual intent, appearance aspirations, performance requirements, 
product quality, etc. only of the Design. The Contractor may use the stated products, having first 
satisfied themselves as to their full compliance with the Specification, performance criteria, as well 
as contractual and Statutory requirements. Alternatively, the Contractor may submit similar 
products for consideration as set out in clause A21.120.4.

11. Prescriptive Elements: A limited number of named elements of the Works that are proprietary 
systems, products or materials, which are to be incorporated in the Works strictly in accordance 
with the manufacturer's recommendations and the Employer's Requirements. If alternative 
systems, products or materials are proposed, the Contractor shall remain responsible for meeting 
the requirements of the Design and such elements should be treated as Descriptive Design.

12. Review Status: The Contractor (including specialist subcontractor) submissions are to be 
reviewed using the A-B-C and D system as described below. The Contractor shall not instruct 
manufacture or installation until A status is achieved for each element of the Works. For clarity the 
status definitions are: 
12.1. A = Reviewed. No comments.
12.2. B = Reviewed. Incorporate comments and resubmit.
12.3. C = Reviewed. Rejected for reasons noted, rework and resubmit.
12.4. D = Not reviewed. Not applicable to the architectural design scope.
12.5. If the Review Status is marked 'A' or 'B' it does not relieve the Contractor/ subcontractor of 

their obligations to comply with the design intent and performance criteria, in any way, 
unless explicitly stated otherwise. Where the submittal is marked 'D' it is incumbent for the 
Contractor to ensure that the correct, qualified and appropriate reviewer is utilised.

13. Samples: Labelled physical products/ materials along with supportive technical information, 
representing elements to be incorporated in the Works, subject to acceptance.

14. Visual Mock-ups: Full size, three dimensional indicative model demonstrating size, orientation, 
shape, etc. manufactured from temporary materials.

15. Prototypes: Full size elements representing the final Detailed Design, incorporating actual 
materials and finishes, but not forming part of the permanent works. Prototypes may be tested to 
confirm compliance with performance requirements.

16. Benchmarks: First section of a pre-identified installation on site for inspection and acceptance.
17. A(a)cceptance/ A(a)pproval/ A(a)pproved: Confirmation that the proposed drawings, submittals 

etc. appear to meet the requirements of the Design and the performance criteria as set out in the 
Specification. It does not relieve the Contractor/subcontractor of their obligations to comply with 
the Specification, relevant Codes and Standards, or any other requirement, unless explicitly stated 
otherwise.

18. Additional Information: Other information necessary to adequately demonstrate Detailed Design 
compliance with the Design, including, but not limited to certification, manufacturers’ information, 
warranties/ guarantees, programme/ delivery information, detailed QA/ QC systems, relevant 
reports and data/ calculations. 

19. Reviewed: The review of a Technical Proposal for non-compliance with the Design.

110 Incomplete documentation 
1. General: Where and to the extent that products or work are not fully documented, they are to be: 

1.1. Of a kind and standard appropriate to the nature and character of that part of the Works 
where they will be used. 

1.2. Suitable for the purposes stated or reasonably to be inferred from the project documents. 
1.3. Contract documents: Omissions or errors in description and/ or quantity shall not vitiate the 

Contract nor release the Contractor from any obligations or liabilities under the Contract. 

120 Workmanship skills 
1. Operatives: Appropriately skilled and experienced for the type and quality of work. 
2. Registration: With Construction Skills Certification Scheme. 
3. Evidence: Operatives must produce evidence of skills/ qualifications when requested. 

130 Quality of products 
1. Standards: Comply with the Standards stated in the Specification. Where stated Standards have 

been superseded/ replaced/ enhanced the version published at the time of Contract award shall 
prevail. Advise at time of Tender any such occurrences. 

2. Generally: All products, materials, components, systems, etc. to be new (unless otherwise agreed 
by the Employer) and compliant with appropriate/ applicable National Standards, Codes of 
Practice and best practice.

3. National Standards/ Codes of Practice: Comply with current Standards, Codes, Planning 
Requirements, etc. Any such documents referred to herein which have been updated or 
superseded, advise at the time of Tender and accommodate the latest publication. 

4. Supply of each product: From the same source or manufacturer. 
Whole quantity of each product required to complete the Works: Consistent kind, size, quality and 
overall appearance.  

5. Tolerances: Where critical, measure a sufficient quantity to determine compliance. Such 
measurement is to be cumulative in nature to ensure completeness.

6. Deterioration: Prevent. Order in suitable quantities to the programme of the Works and use in an 
appropriate sequence.

131 Quality Control 
1. Generally: Comply with BS EN ISO 9001 and ensure compliance with the Specification. 
2. Certification: In accordance with BS EN ISO 9001. 
3. Compatibility: Materials, systems, components and products to be compatible. 
4. Codes and Standards: Comply with current British Standards/ Codes, European Norms and 

relevant By-Laws. Notify changes immediately. 
5. Sustainability: Comply with all sustainability requirements to achieve Client targets. 
6. Storage and Handling Materials:  

6.1. Compliance: With BS 8000. 
6.2. Protection: Protect all items for inclusion in the Works at all times from point of manufacture 

to installation and ensure pristine condition of all elements at handover.
6.3. Pest Infestations: Implement recommendations of BRE Digest 415 - DIG 415 to avoid pest 

infestations.

6.4. Cleaning: Clean all elements thoroughly prior to handover. 
6.5. Defects: Scratches, blemishes, dents, mould, or any other visual defects will not be 

accepted. 
7. Safety: Comply with and demonstrate compliance with CDM and applicable Health and Safety

9.2. Certification: Products and systems to be certified by the British Board of Agrement (BBA).

10. Deleterious Materials: Implement the “Good Practice in the Selection of Construction Materials” 
published by the British Council of Offices. Do not incorporate any of the following in the Works: 

• Asbestos of any kind.
• Wood wool slabs.
• Lead, lead paint or other liquid applications. (Note: Some roofing and rainwater goods 

containing minimal amounts of lead may be acceptable in certain circumstances. Seek 
acceptance prior to ordering).

• Urea formaldehyde foam and any product that may release formaldehyde.
• Polyurethane based insulation.
• Expanded polystyrene and polyurethane.
• Composite metal panels containing an interstitial polyethelene insulated core.
• Chloroflourocarbons and hydrochloroflourocarbons of any kind.
• High alumina cement.
• Tributyltin (TBT).
• Chromated Copper Arsenate (CCA).
• Pentachlorophenol PCP).
• Contaminated gypsum board.
• Pitch polymer DPC.
• Tropical hardwoods.
• Man made or natural mineral fibres that are 3 microns diameter and 200 microns in length, 

to include those that are encapsulated, unsealed and stabilised preventing migration.
• Admixtures containing calcium chloride.
• Polychlorinated biphenyis (PCB) and polychlorinated terphenyis (PCT).
• Lindane wood treatment/ insecticidal spray. 

11. Timber: Procure only from proven and demonstrable sustainable sources, including but not limited 
to: 
11.1. Avoid the use of tropical hardwoods, including veneers, lippings and boards.
11.2. Avoid timber species noted on the IUNC REDLIST listed other than "least concern".
11.3. Timber and timber products to be labeled with the forestry steward ship council (FSC) trade 

mark, programme for the Endorsement of Forest Certification or other acceptable label from 
a recognised independent certification provider. 

11.4. UK Government Timber Procurement Policy.

132 Sustainability 
1. Comply with the Employer’s sustainability and environmental targets and aspirations, which will be 

confirmed in a final as-built review of all performance targets. In addition, achieve the BREEAM, 
WELL and NABERS ratings, which will be confirmed via a final post-construction review. Where 
targets are at risk this must be communicated as soon as possible to the sustainability assessor. 
The BREEAM and WELL pre-assessments have been appended for reference.
 
Carbon is a key target for the client, with upfront and whole life carbon targets for the development. 
All quantities and information must be recorded and provided to the carbon assessor in a timely 
manner. Any products which are likely to exceed the carbon targets must be communicated with 
the carbon assessor as soon as possible and alternative lower-carbon products must be explored. 

2. Comply with the project's Sustainability Report and Sustainability Requirements as set out by 
ElliottWood to achieve the project's target ratings. 
2.1. The Contractor shall notify the Architect of any conflicting requirements, omissions, or 

potential issues between the A-section and Elliott Wood documentation, during the PCSA 
stage. Generally, and in the case of no notification, the most onerous requirement specified 
shall take precedence.

3. Follow the principles of the relevant Standards set out in clause A33.132.1 so that the Detailed 
Design maximises the use of materials, products, systems, techniques, methods etc. which are 
consistent with those Standards and good sustainability practice.

4. With regard material selection and use pay particular attention to the following aspects to 
maximise sustainability rating: 
4.1. Environmental Product Declarations (EPD's) including compliance with BS EN ISO 14025, 

BS EN 15804 and ISO 21930. EPDs to be provided prior to procurement for all systems and 
materials covered within this Specification to inform product selection and whole life carbon 
calculations to be checked against the design stage LCA. 

4.1.1. Where a certified EPD is not available, provide an assessment of the embodied 
carbon of the proposed product.

4.1.2. Supply Health Product Declaration (HDP) for interior finishes and finish materials, 
furnishings, and built-in furniture.

4.2. Durability: All key exposed building elements shall align with the relevant industry durability 
or quality standards as required by BREEAM. 

4.3. Sourcing of materials: All construction materials should be sourced in accordance with the 
project's Sustainable Procurement Plan. Sourcing should be in line with guidance provided 
by BS ISO 20400 and requirements set out in clause A33.131.11.

4.4. Material Efficiency Principles should follow BS8895: Parts 1 to 3.
4.5. Transportation confirmed by a travel plan that minimises delivery miles.
4.6. Waste should be minimised and properly disposed of. Refer to project's Sustainability 

Report for waste targets.
4.7. Prioritise low VOC products, natural materials and minimum standards for product selection 

based on British Standards, and EU standards. Product emission limits, testing 
requirements, evidences and any additional requirements are to be in line with the 
accredited schemes and sustainability credits targeted in the Employer's Requirements.

4.8. Recycled Content of materials and/or products. Refer to project's Sustainability Report for 
recycled content targets.

4.9. Circular economy principles including but not limited to, design for adaptability, design for 
construction, circular materials selection and recourse efficiency. Refer to project's 
Sustainability Report for circular economy targets.

4.10. Climate change risk as defined by the IPCC Assessment Reports.
5. Comply with the requirements outlined in the table below. For further information on targeted 

credits/conditions of each accreditation scheme refer to relevant trackers included in the 
Employer's Requirements.

135 Quality of Execution 
1. Generally: Fix, apply, install or lay products securely, accurately, plumb, neatly and in alignment. 
2. All of the Works to be completed to the satisfaction of the Architect.
3. Compliance: Execute the Works to a very high acceptable standard and comply with: 

• BS 8000
• BS EN ISO 8501: Part 1:
• BS 5493:
• BS EN ISO 12944: Parts 1-8:
• BS EN ISO 14713: 

4. Visible elements: Angles, junctions, edges, corners, arises, component interfaces and all visible 
elements to be tight, smooth, correctly positioned, neat, properly aligned, and ready for use without 
remedial work. 

5. Tolerances: Maintain acceptable tolerances at all times. 
6. Preceding works:  

6.1. Check and ensure accuracy of preceding elements before commencement of installation to 
guarantee accuracy. 

6.2. Do not cut, drill or alter the preceding work or interfacing installations without prior 
authorisation. Any such authorised work shall not change the visual intent. 

7. Sequencing: 
7.1. Implement proper sequencing of elements and components to guarantee quality of final 

installation.
7.2. Ensure relevant work areas/building zones are suitably watertight before commencing 

internal finishes. 
8. Preparation: Surfaces to receive treatment or attachments to be suitably prepared to ensure 

accurate installation without causing any damage or reduction in performance, quality or visual 
intent. 

9. Tools: Minimise use of power activated tools. 
10. Cleaning: 

10.1. Blast cleaning: Blast cleaning to be grade Sa 2.5 after removal of all debris and deposits to 
achieve required surface finish quality. Primer to be applied immediately after blast cleaning. 

10.2. Prior to installation: Remove efflorescence, dirt, dust, traces and any other contaminant prior 
to installation or application of finishes. 

10.3. Handover: Professionally clean the Works prior to handover. 
11. Manufacture: Maximise off site manufacture where specified and appropriate. 
12. Installations: 

12.1. General: Installations to be permanently stable and remain in position without twisting, 
warping, loosening, vibrating, knocking, rattling, whistling, etc. 

12.2. Weather: Do not carry out installations during inclement weather, unless measures have 
been taken to mitigate the effects of low temperature, frost, high rainfall, snow, high 
humidity, etc.

accordance with BS EN 1011. Welding to be smooth, continuous and visually acceptable.

1. System Tolerances:

1.5. Check completed work on site prior to commencing manufacture where possible to check

1.6. Accommodate anticipated structural tolerances and movement. 
1.7. Continually monitor tolerances achieved and keep detailed records. 
1.8. Where tolerance clashes/ discrepancies occur, the most stringent is to be met. 
1.9. Keep records and report deviations/ discrepancies of all tolerances from manufacture to 

installation. 
1.10. Apply the most stringent tolerance requirement when different solutions are determined for 

the same condition. 
1.11. Do not compound tolerances for adjoining elements, each of which is to be treated 

individually. 
1.12. Prepare written methodology adopted to maintain and check tolerances as the work 

proceeds.

351 General structural requirements 
1. Take account of Structural Engineer’s documents and specifications, etc. related to loadings, 

tolerances and design and safely accommodate the worst combination of structural conditions, 
including pressure coefficients. 

2. Take account of BS EN 1991: Part 1. 
3. Ensure rigidity of materials and components so that they are not adversely affected by any 

external or internal vibration and safely withstand all forces and loads imposed at all times. 
4. Install and align all parts of the Work to accommodate deviations of any part of the structure. 
5. Safely accommodate all movement and stresses imposed by the structure as well as all dead/ live 

loads imposed by self weight, attachments, environmental conditions, temporary loads during 
construction, without affecting visual intent. 

Make Limited A33
30-08-2024 Quality standards/ control

Page 16 of 378

6. All movement joints to be visually acceptable. 
7. Components, materials, systems and products to accommodate expansion/ contraction when 

conditions range between 5°C and 28°C air temperature, as well as surface differential 
temperature of between five and eighty degrees centigrade solar radiation. 

8. Working Drawings to show methods by which tolerances of elements, including interfaces, are 
accommodated. 

9. Take into account the following: 
• BS 6180. 
• BS EN 1991 - Eurocode 1.
• BS EN 1991 Parts 1 - 4 (as applicable to location). 9.4. PD 6688. 

10. All elements to resist all static and dynamic loads, without deformation or changes to visual intent, 
including conditions of extreme wind loads, moving elements/ components in close proximity and 
unexpected water absorption. 

11. Take account of all loads imposed during cleaning and maintenance operations including 
operatives, machinery, products, temporary structures and the like to a minimum of 500 Newtons 
applied through 100mm square.

12. All imposed loads to be safely transferred to the adjoining structure, without detrimental effect to 
visual intent, performance or functionality.

13. Accommodate all deflections, including those of a temporary nature, without detrimental effect to 
visual intent, performance or functionality.

14. Maximum allowable deflection, when fully loaded, to be the lower of either 15mm or 1/175 of clear 
span.

15. Accommodate all thermal movements caused by climatic conditions externally and internally. 
Provide necessary permanent/ temporary structural support, as necessary, and take measures to 
mitigate against noise arising.

16. All components, products, materials, fixtures, fittings and systems are to remain rigid under all 
conditions unless purposefully designed otherwise.

17. Maximum permitted deflection to be span/400 (span maximum 1500mm). 

352 General environmental requirements 
1. Take account of Services Engineer’s documents, specifications, Approved Document Part L of the 

Building Regulations, etc. related to local conditions, performance requirements, eradication of 
cold bridging etc. 

2. When selecting materials, components, products, etc. take account of prevailing local conditions to 
ensure suitability for the Design and Service Life of the Works. 

3. All products, materials, components etc. to be moisture and water resistant, including resisting 
changes in moisture content of air or humidity, the effect of freezing and material moisture content. 

4. Protect against frost, precipitation of all kinds, humidity, etc. during manufacture, delivery, storage 
and installation up to handover.

5. Maintain weatherproof and watertight barriers between external and internal conditions, including 
prevention of water ingress, condensation and capillarity at all times and under all conditions.

6. External, internal or interstitial condensation is not acceptable. Comply with BS 5250 and other 
applicable codes or standards with regard condensation calculation methods and demonstrate 
compliance through calculations, testing and adequate detailing.

Regulations (including Parts A and B), including maintaining very low flame spread 
characteristics and the non production of smoke or toxic gases during combustion.

• BS EN 13501: All relevant parts.
• BS EN 13501-2 to 4, Building Regulations related to fire resistance and performance.
• BS7974 - Code of Practice, fire safety engineering.
• BS 9991 - Code of Practice (design/ management/ use).
• BS 9999 - Code of Practice (design/ management/ use).

net/ sheet materials.
• TRADA - Timber Research and Development Association.

Authorities. 
3. Provide test certificates to confirm compliance of all products, materials, components and systems. 
4. Provide all necessary smoke and fire resistant cavity barriers, seals and fire/ smoke stops to 

maintain integrity and insulation, which continue to be effective and in place during a fire.
5. Prevent fire and smoke spreading between fire separation compartments in both floor to floor and 

area to area on individual floors.
6. Provide designated fire fighting enclosures, lift shafts and lobbies that are adequately ventilated, 

incorporating sufficient operable vents being a minimum of 25% of the horizontal cross sectional 
area of each enclosure.

8. Weatherproofing/ Watertightness:

8.3. All external walls/ roofs are to be tested or certified to demonstrate that the building is 

weather and watertight, without leakage or ponding. Any such occurrence to be immediately 
notified, repaired and re-tested. 

9. Flood Testing: 
9.1. Supervised and recorded site flood tests to be actioned, as necessary. Such tests are to be 

carried out by filling the designated area with water to a depth of at least 50mm and 12mm 
below kerb level, after sealing all outlets, joints, junctions, etc. for a minimum period of 48 
hours.

9.2. Depths to be checked every four hours and if lower water levels are recorded, immediately 
locate the leak point, carry out a satisfactory repair and re-test until no drop in levels is 
detected. 

10. Hose testing: Site hose tests to comply with CWCT TN41 - “Site Testing for Watertightness” and 
be carried out on benchmarks and designated areas of the final installation as instructed. 

11. Fire testing:

each type to confirm adequacy.

subsequent Fire Authorities’ requirements.

Testing
410 Compressive strength of mortar for each walling type 
1. Testing authority: A UKAS Accredited laboratory
2. Test method: To BS EN 1015-11.
3. Preliminary tests procedure: As follows: 

3.1. Specimens   
3.1.1. Number of specimens: 6.
3.1.2. Type: 40 x 40 x 160 mm prism.
3.1.3. Preparation: At least six weeks before walling commences.

3.2. Specimen testing: Half of specimens at 7 days. Remainder at 28 days. 
3.2.1. Retarded mixes: Extend curing periods to include retardation period.

3.3. Response to result: If mean compressive strength at 28 days is not within the range given 
below repeat tests with more suitable sand or next higher designation of mortar.

4. Site tests procedure: As follows. 
4.1. Number of specimens: Six per 150m² of walling or per storey whichever the more frequent.
4.2. Specimen types: As preliminary test, but prepared during construction.
4.3. Specimen testing: Half of specimens at 7 days. Remainder at 28 days. 

4.3.1. Retarded mixes: Extend curing periods to include retardation period.
5. Required test mean compressive strength at 28 days (N/mm²): To be agreed.
6. Results: Submit.

415 Fresh mortar cement content 
1. Test method: BREMORTEST in accordance with Building Research Establishment Information 

Paper 8/89
2. Test specimens: Test mortar for the following wall types: F10/ all wall types .
3. Results: Submit.
4. Alternative low embodied carbon mortars:: Contractor to submit proposals.

500 Laying generally

5. Vertical joints in brick and concrete block facework: Even widths. Plumb at every fifth cross joint.

520 Accuracy 
1. Courses: Level and true to line.
2. Faces, angles and features: Plumb.
3. Permissible deviations   

3.1. Position in plan of any point in relation to the specified building reference line and/ or point at 
the same level: ± 10 mm.

3.2. Straightness in any 5m length: ± 5 mm.
3.3. Verticality up to 3 m height : ± 10 mm.
3.4. Verticality up to 7 m height : ± 14 mm.
3.5. Overall thickness of walls: ± 10 mm.
3.6. Level of bed joints up to 5 m (brick masonry): ± 11 mm.
3.7. Level of bed joints up to 5 m (block masonry): ± 13 mm.

690 Adverse weather 
1. General: Do not use frozen materials or lay on frozen surfaces.
2. Air temperature requirements: Do not lay bricks/ blocks: 

2.1. In cement gauged mortars when at or below 3°C and falling or unless it is at least 1°C and 
rising.

2.2. In hydraulic lime:sand mortars when at or below 5°C and falling or below 3°C and rising, or 
as manufacturer’s/ supplier’s recommendations.

2.3. In thin layer mortars when outside the limits set by the mortar manufacturer.
3. Temperature of walling during curing: Above freezing until hardened.
4. Newly erected walling: Protect at all times from: 

4.1. Rain and snow.
4.2. Drying out too rapidly in hot conditions and in drying winds.

Additional requirements for facework
710 The term facework 
1. Definition: Applicable in this specification to block walling finished fair. 

1.1. Painted facework: The only requirement to be waived is that relating to colour.

750 Colour consistency of masonry units 
1. Colour range: Submit proposals of methods taken to ensure that units are of consistent and even 

appearance within deliveries.
2. Conformity: Check each delivery for consistency of appearance with previous deliveries and with 

approved reference panels; do not use if variation is excessive.
3. Facing bricks should be blended on site from a minimum of three packs to ensure an even 

distribution of colour and texture variation.
4. Finished work: Free from patches, horizontal stripes and racking back marks.

760 Appearance 
1. Brick/ block selection: Do not use units with damaged faces or arrises.
2. Cut masonry units: Where cut faces or edges are exposed cut with table masonry saw.
3. Quality control: Lay masonry units to match relevant reference panels. 

3.1. Setting out: To produce satisfactory junctions and joints with built-in features and 
components.

3.2. Coursing: Evenly spaced using gauge rods.
4. Lifts: Complete in one operation.
5. Methods of protecting facework: Submit proposals.

780 Ground level 
1. Commencement of facework: Not less than 150 mm below finished level of adjoining ground or 

external works level.

800 Toothed bond 
1. New and existing facework in same plane: Bond together at every course to achieve continuity.

2. Mortar on facework: Allow to dry before removing with stiff bristled brush.

360 Preparation of surrounds

1.1. A thick application of bituminous emulsion paint applied to avoid the areas near jambs, 
which would result in contact with perimeter sealant. Allow emulsion to dry before placing 
mortar bed.

1.2. A strip of reinforced bitumen membrane in accordance with BS 8747 Class S1P1, S2P3 or 
S4P4.

1.3. A strip of polyethylene damp proof course to BS 6515.

380 Laying glass blocks with mortar joints

5. Accuracy: Courses to be level and true to line. Faces, angles and features to be plumb.

3. Setting out: Achieve satisfactory junctions with surrounds, built in elements and components.
4. Accuracy: Courses to be level and true to line. Faces, angles and features to be plumb.

390 Installing joint reinforcement 
1. Reinforcement: A type recommended for the purpose by glass block manufacturer/ supplier.
2. Placement: Continuously, embedded in joints maintaining not less than 20 mm from glass block 

faces. Form around changes in direction. Lap joints in length not less than 150 mm and tie where 
recommended by manufacturer/ supplier. Do not restrict movement at perimeter and expansion 
joints.

420 Use of spacers 
1. Type: As recommended for the purpose by glass block manufacturer/ supplier.
2. Placement: Build into each mortar joint intersection as work proceeds.

430 Execution of mortar jointing 
1. Joints: Set back 2 mm (nominal) from face of glass blocks.
2. Cleanliness: Remove surplus mortar from glass block faces as jointing proceeds. Leave no mortar 

residue on face of blocks.

435 Execution of mortar pointing 
1. Jointing mortar: Rake back 10 mm from glass block faces as laying proceeds and allow to set.
2. Preparation: Brush out joints and lightly wet.
3. Placement: Point joints in a continuous operation, keeping 2 mm (nominal) back from face of glass 

blocks.
4. Cleanliness: Remove surplus mortar from glass block faces before final set. Leave no mortar 

residue on face of blocks.

437 Execution of sealant pointing 
1. Jointing mortar: Rake back 10 mm from glass block faces as laying proceeds and allow to set.
2. Preparation: Remove all loose debris, then as section Z22.
3. Placement: As section Z22 keeping 2 mm (nominal) back from face of glass blocks.
4. Cleanliness: Remove surplus sealant from glass block surfaces as work proceeds.

2. Air temperature requirements: Do not apply coatings when at or below 5°C and falling, or below 

3°C and rising.
3. Temperature of work: Maintain above 5°C until coatings have hardened sufficiently.

Preparing substrates
410 Suitability of substrates 
1. Preparation generally: To tanking mortar/ admixture manufacturer’s recommendations.
2. Stability and soundness: Free from movement, and loose or weak areas that will cause failure of 

tanking.
3. Key: To achieve firm adhesion of tanking.
4. Contamination: Free from previous coatings and contaminants including dirt, dust, efflorescence, 

mould, oil, paint and plaster.
5. Cracks, porous patches and other defective areas subject to water pressure and liable to admit 

water: Control and seal using waterproof mortar recommended by the tanking mortar 
manufacturer.

6. Holes/ recesses for fixings (where permitted): Prepared to receive fasteners.
7. Openings and chases: Prepared, including sleeves for pipe penetrations and chases to receive 

waterproofing compounds/ sealants.

420 Preparation of mortar joints and cavities 
1. Mortar joints: Rake out to a depth of 12 mm (minimum). 

1.1. Debris: Remove and flush out with water.
1.2. Fill: Repoint with waterproof mortar to the tanking mortar manufacturer's recommendations.

2. Blow holes, cavities, cracks, etc: Remove loose material and fill flush using waterproof mortar 
recommended by the tanking mortar manufacturer.

430 Tanking integrity 
1. Penetrations for fixings, services, etc: Not permitted.

Execution
510 Application generally 
1. Application methods and coating sequence: As recommended by the tanking mortar/ admixture 

manufacturer to achieve a water-resistant structure.

520 Joints/ junctions and penetrations 
1. Abutments, joints and active cracks: Sealed and watertight. 

1.1. Movement joints: Centred over joints in substrate and extended through waterproof coating 
and finishes.

1.2. Daywork joints in successive coatings: Staggered and lapped.
1.3. Angled joints: Not permitted.

2. Penetrations: Watertight.

530 Appearance of tanking 
1. Render/ screed coatings: Even and consistent. Free from rippling, hollows, ridges, cracks and 

crazing. 
1.1. Accuracy: A true plane, to correct line and level. Walls and reveals plumb and square with 

neat arrises
2. Thin slurry coatings: Consistent and free from hollows, cracks and crazing. Suitable to receive 

specified finish.

540 Flatness/ surface regularity of tanking screeds 
1. Measurement method: From the underside of a 3 m straight edge (between points of contact) 

placed anywhere on surface and using a slip gauge to BS 8204-1 or -2 (or equivalent).
2. Deviation of surface: Sudden irregularities not permitted. 

2.1. Maximum permissible deviation: 5 mm and 2 mm under a 1 m straight edge.

550 Curing and drying 
1. General: Prevent premature setting, uneven drying and cracking of each coat.
2. Curing coatings: Prevent evaporation from surface. 

2.1. Curing period (minimum): As the tanking mortar manufacturer's recommendations.
As the admixture manufacturer's recommendations.

• Boards in good condition, well-fitting and stable. 
• Cover to prevent wind uplift and flotation as soon as practicable.

840 Laying stone ballast 
1. Condition of substrate: Clean.
2. Gravel guards: Fit to outlets.
3. Substrate: Protect substrate surface from ballast.
4. Laying: Spread evenly. Do not pile to excessive heights. 

4.1. Depth (minimum): As roof system manufacturers recommendations and system 
requirements.

Completion
910 Inspection 
1. Coating surfaces: Check when cured for discontinuities. 

1.1. Defective areas: Apply another coating.

1. Testing authority: Submit proposals
2. Timing of test: Give notice. Before laying insulation and ballasting.
3. Condition of roof prior to testing   

3.1. Coating: Complete to a stage where integrity can be tested.
3.2. Surface: Clean.

4. Test results: Submit.
5. Waterproof integrity certificate: Submit, on completion of testing.

930 Flood test 
1. Condition of roof prior to testing   

1.1. Coating: Complete to a stage where integrity can be tested.
1.2. Surface: Clean.

2. Outlets: Externally cover and seal. Protect against damage from water pressure using temporary 
kerbs. Do not use plugs to seal outlets.

3. Flood levels: Refer to the A sections. In no case higher than existing kerbs.
4. Flood duration: Refer to the A sections.

Undertake wind uplift calculations in accordance with the designated code of practice to ensure the

nuisance from interstitial condensation do not occur.

Waterproof membranes/ accessories
710 Laying reinforced bitumen membranes generally 
1. Direction of laying: Unrolled up the slope. 

1.1. Where practicable, install so that water drains over and not into laps.
2. Side and end laps: As recommended by bitumen membrane manufacturer
3. Head and side laps: Offset.
4. Intermediate and top layer/ capsheet: Fully bond.
5. Successive layers: Apply without delay. Do not trap moisture.
6. Strips of bitumen membrane for 'linear' details: Cut from length of roll.
7. Completed coverings: Firmly attached, fully sealed, smooth, weatherproof and free-draining.

740 Torch-on bonding of reinforced bitumen membranes 
1. Bond: Full over whole surface, with no air pockets.
2. Excess compound at laps of top layer/ capsheet: Leave as continuous bead.

745 Cold-applied adhesive bonding of reinforced bitumen membranes 
1. Bond: Full over whole surface, with no air pockets.

750 Laying mineral-faced reinforced bitumen membranes 
1. Lap positions and detailing of ridges, eaves, verges, hips, abutments, etc: Submit proposals.
2. Setting out: Neat, with carefully formed junctions.
3. Lap bonding: Carry out only at prefinished margins or prepared 'black to black' edges.
4. Excess bonding compound at laps: Remove whilst still warm.

775 Skirtings and upstands 
1. Angle fillets: As per manufacturer's recommendation.
2. Venting first layer of bitumen membrane: As per manufacturer's recommendation.
3. Other layers of bitumen membrane: As per manufacturer's recommendation.
4. Upstands   

4.1. At ends of rolls: Form with bitumen membrane carried up without using separate strip.
4.2. Elsewhere: Form with matching strips of bitumen membrane, maintaining laps.
4.3. Additional fixing of bitumen membranes: As recommended by bitumen membrane 

manufacturer.

930 Flood test

General/ preparation
305 Compliance with performance requirements 
1. Testing/ Assessment: Submit UKAS accredited laboratory reports for the following: Fire resistance:

units).

2. Materials, components and details: As used in testing/ assessment reports. If discrepancies arise, 
give notice.

3. Linings and Partitions to consist of complete integrated systems and maintain performance from 
floor slab to soffit.

4. Entire system should be from one supplier, no mixing/ matching permitted.

325 Preparation of masonry to receive wall linings 
1. General: Suitable to receive lining system. Redundant fixtures and services removed. Cutting, 

chasing and making good completed.
2. Holes, gaps, service penetrations, perimeter junctions and around openings: Seal.
3. Adhesive fixings: Prepare substrate to achieve effective bonding. 

3.1. Contaminants: Remove loose material, dirt, grease, oil, paper, etc.
3.2. Absorption: Control by dampening, priming or applying bonding agents as necessary.

335 Additional supports 
1. Framing: Accurately position and securely fix to give full support to: 

1.1. Partition heads running parallel with, but offset from main structural supports.
1.2. Fixtures, fittings and service outlets. Mark framing positions clearly and accurately on 

linings.
1.3. Board edges and lining perimeters, as recommended by board manufacturer to suit type 

and performance of lining.
2. Backing board:   

2.1. Use proprietary tile backing board as outer board where the system will receive a weight >= 
32kg/m².

2.2. Performance of the overall system to be maintained.

Installation
435 Dry linings generally 
1. General: Use fixing, jointing, sealing and finishing materials, components and installation methods 

recommended by board manufacturer.
2. Cutting gypsum boards: Neatly and accurately without damaging core or tearing paper facing. 

2.1. Cut edges: Minimize and position at internal angles wherever possible. Mask with bound 
edges of adjacent boards at external corners.

3. Fixings boards: Securely and firmly to suitably prepared and accurately levelled backgrounds.
4. Finishing: Neatly to give flush, smooth, flat surfaces free from bowing and abrupt changes of level.
5. Wet or Humid conditions: Outer layer of gypsum board to be moisture resistant with appropriate 

performance characteristics to suit the system. 

445 Ceilings 
1. Sequence: Fix boards to ceilings before installing dry lined walls and partitions.
2. Orientation of boards: Fix with bound edges at right angles to supports and with ends staggered in 

adjacent rows.
3. Two layer boarding: Stagger joints between layers.

455 Metal framing for partitions/ wall linings 
1. Setting out: Accurately aligned and plumb. 

1.1. Frame/ Stud positions: Equal centres to suit specified linings, maintaining sequence across 
openings.

1.2. Additional studs: To support vertical edges of boards.
2. Fixing centres at perimeters (maximum): 600 mm maximum, reduce centres where required to suit 

the service conditions.
3. Fixings: Securely back to suitable structure accommodating all deflections.  

4. Openings: Form and seal accurately with a water based acrylic mastic fire seal without detriment 
to the appearance or performance of the system. 
4.1. Doorsets: Use sleeved or boxed metal studs and/ or suitable timber framing to achieve 

strength grade requirements for framing assembly and adequately support weight of door.
4.2. Services penetrations: Allow for associated fire-stopping.

465 Staggered stud partitions 
1. Horizontal frame members (noggins, bearers, etc.) and boards: Fix between alternate studs and 

not touching adjacent offset studs.

475 Metal furrings for wall linings 
1. Setting out: Accurately aligned and plumb.

1.1. Vertical furring positions: Equal vertical centres to suit specified linings, maintaining

1.2. Additional vertical furrings: To support vertical edges of boards and at junctions with

1.3. Horizontal furring positions: To provide continuous support to edges of boards.

485 Suspended ceiling grids

505 Installing mineral wool insulation

displacement.
2. Services   

2.1. Electrical cables overlaid by insulation: Sized accordingly.
2.2. Ceilings: Cut insulation around electrical fittings, etc.

510 Sealing gaps and air paths 
1. Location of sealant: To perimeter abutments and around openings. 

1.1. Pressurized shafts and ducts: At board-to-board and board-to-metal frame junctions.
2. Application: To clean, dry and dust free surfaces as a continuous bead with no gaps. 

2.1. Gaps greater than 6 mm between floor and underside of gypsum board: After sealing, fill 
with jointing compound.

530 Cavity fire barriers within partitions/ wall linings 
1. Metal framed systems   

1.1. Material: Plasterboard 12.5 mm thick.
1.2. Installation: Form accurately and fix securely with no gaps to provide a complete barrier to 

smoke and flame.
2. Adhesive fixed wall lining systems   

2.1. Material: Adhesive compound.
2.2. Installation: Form in a continuous line with no gaps to provide a complete barrier to smoke 

and flame.

545 Cavity fire barriers within suspended ceilings 
1. Type: As recommended by board manufacturer to meet specified performance
2. Fire resistance: To meet specified performance
3. Ceiling void subdivision: Fix barriers as drawings.
4. Fixing at perimeters and joints: Secure, stable and continuous with no gaps, to provide a complete 

barrier to smoke and flame.

5. Service penetrations: Cut and pack to maintain barrier integrity. Sleeve flexible materials.

6. Ceiling systems for fire protection: Do not impair fire-resisting performance of ceiling system.

555 Fire-stopping at perimeters of dry lining systems 
1. Material: Tightly packed mineral wool or intumescent mastic/ sealant.
2. Application: To perimeter abutments to provide a complete barrier to smoke and flame.

556 Fire-stopping and barriers generally 
1. Comply with BS EN 1364 and BS EN 1366.
2. Fire/acoustic barriers, seals, strips: To be non-visible and appropriate at all conditions whilst 

maintaining performance. 

560 Joints between boards 
1. Tapered edged gypsum boards   

1.1. Bound edges: Lightly butted.
1.2. Cut/ unbound edges: 3 mm gap maximum.

2. Square edged plasterboards: 3 mm gap maximum.
3. Square edged gypsum fibre boards: 5 mm gap maximum.
4. Movement Joints: Invisible. 

565 Vertical joints 
1. Joints: Centre on studs. 

1.1. Partitions: Stagger joints on opposite sides of studs.
1.2. Two layer boarding: Stagger joints between layers.

570 Horizontal joints 
1. Surfaces exposed to view: Horizontal joints not permitted. Seek instructions where height of 

partition/ lining exceeds maximum available length of board.
2. Two layer boarding: Stagger joints between layers by at least 600 mm.
3. Edges of boards: Support using additional framing. 

3.1. Two layer boarding: Support edges of outer layer.

575 Plank plasterboard 
1. First layer in two layer boarding: Square edged with long edges at right angles to studs.

580 Insulation backed plasterboard 
1. General: Do not damage or cut away insulation to accommodate services.
2. Installation at corners: Carefully cut back insulation or plasterboard as appropriate along edges of 

boards to give a continuous plasterboard face, with no gaps in insulation.

590 Fixing gypsum board to metal framing/ Furrings 
1. Partitions/ Wall linings: Fix securely and firmly at the following centres (maximum): 

1.1. Single layer boarding: To all framing at 300 mm centres. Reduce to 200 mm centres at 
external angles.

1.2. Multi-layer boarding: Face layer at 300 mm centres, and previous layers around perimeters 
at 300 mm centres.

2. Ceilings: 230 mm. Reduce to 150 mm at board ends and at lining perimeters.

01061 - Jermyn Street Piccadilly – Detailed Architectural Specification - Detailed Revisions
Client: GPE

3. Position of screws from edges of boards (minimum): 10 mm. 
3.1. Screw heads: Set in a depression. Do not break paper or gypsum core.

592 Fixing insulation backed plasterboard to metal furrings 
1. Fixing to furrings: In addition to screw fixings apply continuous beads of adhesive sealant to 

furrings.

595 Deflection heads 
1. Fixing boards: Do not fix to head channels.
2. Provide: Deflection details of all heads and abutments to suit deflection calculations and service 

conditions. 
3. Visibility: All works to be concealed. 
4. Documentation: Refer to Structural Engineers documentation. 

Finishing
640 Miscellaneous 
1. Sockets/ Outlets/ Data boxes/ Switches:  

1.1. Do not locate back to back.
1.2. Detailing as per manufacturer's recommendations. 
1.3. Include necessary acoustic and putty pads where required.

2. Plates/ Patreses/ Noggings: Integrate within the system as required to support fixtures and fittings. 
3. Loadings: Where partition / loading will receive wall tiling with a weight greater than 31kg/m2, tile 

backer boards to be used.  
4. Access Panels: Included within systems whilst maintaining performance in locations agreed. 

650 Level of dry lining across joints 
1. Sudden irregularities: Not permitted.
2. Joint deviations: Measure from faces of adjacent boards using methods and straightedges (450 

mm long with feet/ pads) to BS 8212, clause 3.3.5. 
2.1. Tapered edge joints   

2.1.1. Permissible deviation (maximum) across joints when measured with feet resting on 
boards: 3 mm.

2.2. External angles   
2.2.1. Permissible deviation (maximum) for both faces: 4 mm.

2.3. Internal angles   
2.3.1. Permissible deviation (maximum) for both faces: 5 mm.

670 Seamless jointing to gypsum boards 
1. Cut edges of boards: Lightly sand to remove paper burrs.
2. Filling and taping: Fill joints, gaps and internal angles with jointing compound and cover with 

continuous lengths of paper tape, fully bedded.
3. Protection of edges/ corners: Reinforce external angles, stop ends, etc. with specified edge/ angle 

bead.
4. Finishing: Apply jointing compound. Feather out each application beyond previous application to 

give a flush, smooth, seamless surface.
5. Nail/ screw depressions: Fill with jointing compound to give a flush surface.
6. Minor imperfections: Remove by light sanding.

320 Moisture content of new concrete/ Screed substrates for floating floors 
1. Test for moisture content

service condition until Completion.

and seal to top face of boards using suitable systems in accordance with manufacturers details 
and recommendations. 
3.1. Excess material: Trim off neatly after fixing skirting boards/ cover beads.

4. Membrane condition: Intact, clean and dry prior to laying flooring.

350 Treated timber

3. Timber movement: Position boards and fixings to prevent cupping, springing, excessive opening of 

joints and other defects.
4. Heading joints: Tightly butted, central over supports and at least two board widths apart on any 

one support.

4.4. Metal roof decking: Fix to sides of liner tray corrugations.
4.5. Timber: Fix to side of joists at least 50 mm from bottom edge. If ceiling system is intended 

for fire protection, fix into top third of joists.
4.6. Hollow structural members: Submit fixing proposals.

325 Installing hangers 
1. Wire hangers: Straighten and tension before use.
2. Installation: Install vertical or near vertical, without bends or kinks. Do not allow hangers to press 

against fittings, services, or insulation covering ducts/ pipes.
3. Obstructions: Where obstructions prevent vertical installation, either brace diagonal hangers 

against lateral movement, or hang ceiling system on an appropriate rigid sub-grid bridging across 
obstructions and supported to prevent lateral movement.

4. Extra hangers: Provide as necessary to carry additional loads.
5. Fixing   

5.1. Wire hangers: Tie securely at top with tight bends to loops to prevent vertical movement.
5.2. Angle/ strap hangers: Do not use rivets for top fixing.

6. Spacings: To be confirmed.

1. Main runners: Install level. Do not kink or bend hangers. 

1.1. Spliced joints: Stagger.
1.2. Wire hangers passing through main runners: Use sharp bends and tightly wrapped loops.
1.3. Angle/ strap hangers: Do not use rivets for bottom fixing.
1.4. Angular displacement of long axis of one runner in relation to next runner in line with it: Not 

visually apparent.
2. Cross members supported by main runners or other cross members: Install perpendicular to 

intersecting runners.
3. Cross tees: Flat and coplanar with flanges of main runners after panel insertion. 

3.1. Cross tees over 600 mm long, cut and resting on perimeter trim: Provide an additional 
hanger.

4. Holding down clips: Locate to manufacturer's recommendations. 
4.1. Fire-protecting/ resisting ceiling systems: Use clip type featured in the fire test/ assessment.

345 Concealed grids 
1. Primary support channels: Install level. Do not kink or bend hangers. 

1.1. Wire hangers wrapped around primary channels: Twice wrapped. Loops tightly formed.
1.2. Angle/ Strap hangers: Do not use rivets for bottom fixing.

2. Splines: Locate between infill units to assist levelling of adjacent units and to resist air movement 
at joints.

3. Spring-tee grids: Do not omit primary channel.

355 Installing infill units 
1. General   

1.1. Perimeter infill units: Trimmed, as necessary, to fully fill space between last grid member 
and perimeter trim. Prevent subsequent movement.

1.2. Deeply textured infill units: Minimize variations in apparent texture and colour. In particular, 
avoid patchiness.

2. Concealed grids: Install infill units uniformly, straight and aligned. Avoid dimension creep. 
2.1. Infill units around recessed luminaires and similar openings: Prevent movement and 

displacement.

365 Installing metal infill units 
1. Sound absorbing pads: Fit to prevent upward air movement through infill units. Cut or fold pads in 

cut perimeter infill units to full unit size. Reseal cut pads.
2. Perimeter infill units: Firmly wedge cut units into perimeter trim, or clip down.

375 Board ceiling systems 
1. Cut boards: Neat and accurate.
2. Fixing to grid   

2.1. Board edges: Fully support. Screw to grid members. Set heads of screws below surface of 
boards and fill flush with surface.

2.2. Boards applied in two or more layers: Stagger joints.
Make Limited K40

confirmed..

2.2. Linear air diffusers: Retain in place with lateral restraint. Provide flanges for support of grid

415 Installing insulation 
1. Fitting: Fit accurately and firmly with butted joints and no gaps.
2. Insulation within individual infill units: Fit closely. Secure to prevent displacement when infill units 

are installed or subsequently lifted.

carefully around electrical fittings, etc. Do not lay insulation over luminaires.
5. Sloping and vertical areas of ceiling system: Fasten insulation, to prevent displacement.

421 Ceiling systems intended for fire protection 
1. Junctions of ceiling systems with perimeter abutments and service penetrations: Seal gaps with 

tightly packed mineral wool or intumescent sealant to prevent penetration of smoke and flame.
2. Ceiling system/ wall junctions: Maintain protective value of ceiling system. 

2.1. Fixings and grounds: Noncombustible.
2.2. Metal trim: Provide for thermal expansion.

3. Access and access panels: Maintain continuity of fire protection.

425 Installing cavity fire barriers 
1. Maximum ceiling void dimension in any direction: To be confirmed.
2. Fixing:   

2.1. General: Fix barriers securely to channels or angles at abutments to building structure.
2.2. At perimeters and joints: Provide permanent stability and continuity with no gaps to form a 

complete barrier to smoke and flame.
3. Joints: Form to preserve integrity in fire.
4. Service penetrations: Cut barriers neatly to accommodate services. Fit fire-resistant sleeves 

around flexible materials. Fill gaps around services to fire barrier manufacturer's recommendations 
to maintain barrier integrity. Adequately support services passing through the barrier.

5. Ceiling systems intended for fire protection: Do not impair fire-resisting performance of ceiling 
system.

6. Ceiling systems not intended for fire protection: Do not mechanically interlink barriers with ceiling 
system.

431 Installing sound barriers 
1. Material: To be confirmed.
2. Setting out: Align accurately with partition heads.
3. Fixing: Fix tightly at perimeters and joints using methods recommended by barrier manufacturer, 

including steel support sections as appropriate. Completed installation to be stable, secure and 
continuous, with no gaps.

4. Gaps at junctions with partition heads, ceiling system, structural soffit, walls, ducts, pipes, 
etc.: Seal with mineral wool or suitable sealant.

L20
Doors/ hatches/ access panels
General
100 Design Compliance 
1. Section to be read in conjunction with the A and Z Sections which form part of the Specification 

and Contract Conditions.
2. Section to be read in conjunction with Work Section L40.
3. Design: Refer to and comply with A10.120 and A21.140.

110 Evidence of performance 
1. Certification: Provide independently certified evidence that all incorporated components comply 

with specified performance requirements.

112 Timber procurement 
1. Refer to section A33.:  

115 Fire resisting and smoke control pedestrian doors/ door assemblies/ 
doorsets 
1. CE marked fire resisting and smoke control pedestrian doorsets: To BS EN 16034 and in 

conjunction with BS EN 13241 and BS EN 14351-1 (and eventually prEN 14351-2).
2. Door products: As defined in BS EN 12519.
3. Evidence of fire performance: Provide certified evidence, in the form of a product conformity 

certificate, directly relevant fire test report or engineering assessment, that each door/ door 
assembly/ doorset supplied will comply with the specified requirements for fire resisting and/ or 
smoke control if tested to BS 476-22, BS EN 1634-1, BS EN 1634-3 or is CE marked to BS EN 
16034. Specified values should not be a combination of both standards. Such certification must 
cover door and frame materials, glass and glazing materials and their installation, essential and 
ancillary ironmongery, hinges and seals.

4. Components, assemblies or sets will be marked to the relevant CE marking European product 
standard (hEN), national product standard and/ or third party certification rating.

120 Site dimensions 
1. Procedure: Before starting work on designated items take site dimensions, record on shop 

drawings and use to ensure accurate fabrication.
2. Designated items: To be agreed with the Architect.

130 Door Operations Generally 
1. Specialist sub-contractor to ensure that the opening force required for operation of all manually 

operated doors (excluding riser doors) is not more than 30N at the leading edge from 0° (the door 
in the closed position) to 30° open, and not more than 22.5N at the leading edge from 30° to 60° of 
the opening cycle in accordance with Building Regulations requirements.

2. Specialist sub-contractor to identify any manually operated doors that exceed this requirement, 
make the necessary provisions to incorporate power assisted opening and confirm requirements 
with both the Architect and the Services Engineer.

3. Manufacturer: Submit proposals

5. Performance: Fire and acoustic rating to suit requirements.

B).
5.2. Acoustic rating: N/A.

absorption Class B to match LIN-170/ K13 - To be confirmed by the Acoustic 
Engineer.

5.3. Duty: Heavy.

6.1. Type: Rebated split frame, as the Design Drawings.

6.4. Appearance: Visually to match LIN-170/ K13.

6.5.1. Rear side: To be co-ordinated with interfacing walls and finishes.
6.5.2. Front side: No architrave, frame co-ordinated and expressed within arrangement of 

LIN-170/ K13.

7.1. Core: Solid laminated timber.
7.2. Lippings: Concealed hardwood lippings to all edges, applied before application of the 

painting overlayment.
7.3. Facings: Vary, as described below. 

7.3.1. Rear side:

7.3.1.1.1. Visual co-ordination: Veneer pattern to be co-ordinated and visually 
consistent with LIN-170/ K13 when door is in open position.

7.3.1.2. Finish: To match LIN-170/ K13.

640 Installation generally 
1. Fasteners and methods of fixing: To section Z20.
2. Structural members: Do not modify, cut, notch or make holes in structural members, except as 

indicated on drawings.
3. Temporary support: Do not use stairs, walkways or balustrades as temporary support or strutting 

for other work.
4. Applied finishes: Substrates to be even, dry, sound and free from contaminants. Make good 

substrate surfaces and prepare/ prime as finish manufacturer’s recommendation before 
application.

5. Visible fixings: Stainless steel hex socket machine (Allen) screws, countersunk where possible.
6. Tolerances:   

6.1. Squareness: No diagonal panel deviation of ±3mm or ±0.075%., whichever is the lowest.
6.2. Levelness: ±2mm from 2m straight edge.
6.3. Flatness: ±2mm from 2m straight edge.
6.4. Vertical joints: < 1.5mm from another joint.
6.5. Vertical plane: <1 in 1000. Theoretical position ±1.5mm. 
6.6. Bow: 3mm or 0.075% whichever is the lowest.
6.7. Twist: <1° from either end.
6.8. Alignment: ±2mm of other elements. 

670 Installation of tread inserts/ nosings 
1. Treads: Fully cured, sound and level.

140 Material samples

150 Workmanship and positioning generally 
1. Glazing generally: In accordance with BS 6262 series.
2. Integrity: Glazing must be wind and watertight under all conditions with full allowance made for 

deflections and other movements.
3. Dimensional tolerances: Panes/ sheets to be within +/-2 mm of specified dimensions.
4. Materials   

4.1. Compatibility: Glass/ plastics, surround materials, sealers, primers and paints/ clear finishes 
to be used together to be compatible. Avoid contact between glazing panes/ units and 
alkaline materials such as cement and lime.

4.2. Protection: Keep materials dry until fixed. Protect insulating glass units and plastics glazing 
sheets from the sun and other heat sources.

152 Preparation 
1. Surrounds, rebates, grooves and beads: Clean and prepare before installing glazing; ensure 

compliance with any certified installation requirements.

155 Glass generally 
1. Standards: To BS 952-1 and the relevant parts of: 

1.1. BS EN 572-9 for basic soda lime silicate glass.
1.2. BS EN 1748-1-1 for borosilicate glass.
1.3. BS EN 1748-2-1  for ceramic glass.
1.4. BS EN 1863-2 for heat-strengthened soda lime silicate glass.
1.5. BS EN 12150-2 for thermally toughened soda lime silicate safety glass.
1.6. BS EN 12337 for chemically strengthened soda lime silicate glass.
1.7. BS EN 13024-2 for thermally toughened borosilicate safety glass.
1.8. BS EN 14449 for laminated glass and laminated safety glass.

2. Panes/ sheets: Clean and free from obvious scratches, bubbles, cracks, rippling, dimples and 
other defects. 
2.1. Edges: Generally undamaged. Shells and chips not more than 2 mm deep and extending 

not more than 5 mm across the surface are acceptable if ground out.

520 Wood floated finish 
1. Finish: Slightly coarse, even texture with no ridges or steps.

530 Smooth floated finish 
1. Finish: Even texture with no ridges or steps.

540 Trowelled finish to levelling screeds 
1. Floating: To an even texture with no ridges or steps.
2. Trowelling: To a uniform, smooth but not polished surface, free from trowel marks and other 

blemishes, and suitable to receive specified flooring material.

550 Trowelled finish to wearing screeds 
1. Floating: To an even texture with no ridges or steps.
2. Trowelling: Successively trowel at intervals, applying sufficient pressure to close surface and give 

a uniform smooth finish free from trowel marks and other blemishes.

560 Dewatered trowelled finish to wearing screeds 
1. Dewatering: Immediately after compaction of wearing screeds, remove water using a vacuum 

process.
2. Floating: Without delay, power float to an even texture with no ridges or steps.
3. Trowelling: Successively trowel at intervals, applying sufficient pressure to close surface and give 

a uniform smooth finish free from trowel marks and other blemishes.

600 Power ground finish to wearing screeds 
1. Floating: To an even surface with no ridges or steps. Immediately commence curing.
2. Grinding: When concrete is sufficiently hard for sand particles not to be torn from surface, remove 

1 - 2 mm from surface to give an even glass-paper texture, free from blemishes and trowel marks.
3. Cleaning: Remove dust and wash down. Resume curing without delay.

650 Curing 
1. General: Prevent premature drying. Immediately after laying, protect surface from wind, draughts 

and strong sunlight. As soon as screed has set sufficiently, closely cover with polyethylene 
sheeting.

2. Curing period (minimum): Keep polyethylene sheeting in position for: period recommended by 
screed manufacturer.

3. Drying after curing: Allow screeds to dry gradually. Do not subject screeds to artificial drying 
conditions that will cause cracking or other shrinkage related problems.

670 Roof screeds 
1. Protection: Cover screeds during wet weather. When weathertight coverings are laid, screeds 

must be as dry as practicable.

370 New in situ concrete

430 Spatterdashing for key

1.1. Cement: Portland to BS EN 197-1 type CEM I/42.5
Make Limited M40

1.2. Sand: Clean coarse.
1.3. Proportions (cement:sand): 1-1.5:2.
1.4. Admixture: BBA certified SBR bonding agent.
1.5. Consistency: Thick slurry with no segregation.

2. Application/finish: Thrown. 
2.1. Thickness: 3-5 mm.

1.2. Sand: Clean coarse.

1.4. Admixture: BBA certified SBR bonding agent.

2. Application/ finish: Brush applied to a deep close texture.
3. Curing/ drying: Keep damp until hardened. Dry out to provide securely bonded finish.

438 Preparing concrete bases for fully bonded bedding 
1. Surface cement:sand matrix: Remove to expose coarse aggregate.
2. Surface preparation: Suitable to achieve a full bond with bedding. Select from: 

2.1. Keep well wetted for several hours. Remove free water then brush in a slurry bonding coat. 
2.1.1. Slurry: SBR polymer modified cement.

2.2. Prepare, prime as necessary and apply a bonding agent. 
2.2.1. Bonding agent: BBA certified SBR bonding agent.

530 Setting out 
1. Joints: True to line, continuous and without steps. 

1.1. Joints on walls: Horizontal, vertical and aligned round corners.

4. Joints in adjoining floors and skirtings: Align.

540 Level of floor tiling 
1. Permissible deviation in level from datum 5mm.

550 Flatness/ Regularity of tiling/ mosaics 
1. Sudden irregularities: Not permitted.
2. Deviation of surface: Measure from underside of a 2 m straightedge with 3 mm thick feet placed 

anywhere on surface. The straightedge should not be obstructed by the tiles and no gap should be 
greater than 6 mm, i.e. a tolerance of

560 Level of tiling across joints 
1. Deviation (maximum) between tile surfaces either side of any type of joint   

1.1. 1 mm for joints less than 6 mm wide.
1.2. 2 mm for joints 6 mm or greater in width.

570 Mortar bedding 
1. Bedding mix   

1.1. Cement: Portland to BS EN 197-1, type CEM I/42.5.
1.2. Sand for walls: Fine aggregate to BS EN 13139. 

1.2.1. Grading designation: 0/2 (CP or MP) category 2 fines.
1.3. Sand for floors: Fine aggregate to BS EN 13139. 

1.3.1. Grading designation: 0/4 (MP) category 1 fines and between 20%-66% passing a 0.5 
sieve.

2. Batching: Select from: 
2.1. Batch by weight.
2.2. Batch by volume: Permitted on the basis of previously established weight:volume 

relationships of the particular materials. Use accurate gauge boxes. Allow for bulking of 
damp sand.

3. Mixing: Mix materials thoroughly to uniform consistence. Use a suitable forced action mechanical 
mixer. Do not use a free fall type mixer.

4. Application: At normal temperatures use within two hours. Do not use after initial set. Do not 
retemper.

01061 - Jermyn Street Piccadilly – Detailed Architectural Specification - Detailed Revisions
Client: GPE

578 Crack control reinforcement 
1. Type to BS 4483: To be confirmed.
2. Installation: Place centrally in depth of bed. Lap not less than 100 mm and securely tie together 

with steel wire.
3. Corners: Avoid a four layer build at corners.

580 Porous tiles 
1. Tiles to be bedded in cement:sand: Soak in clean water for at least 30 minutes. Fix as soon as 

surface water has drained.

590 Coved tile skirtings 
1. Sequence: Bed solid to wall before laying floor tiles.
2. Bedding: 1:4 cement:sand mortar.

600 Sit-On tile skirtings 
1. Sequence: Bed solid to wall after laying floor tiles.
2. Bedding: 1:4 cement:sand mortar.

610 Multistorey external tiling 
1. Sequence: Start fixing at the highest level and work downwards. Protect exposed top edges.
2. Copings, sills, etc.: Complete as soon as practicable.

650 Adhesive bed – notched trowel method (walls) 
1. Application: By 3 mm floated coat of adhesive to dry background in areas of approximately 1 m². 

Comb surface.
2. Tiling: Press tiles firmly onto float coat.

651 Adhesive bed – notched trowel and buttering method (walls) 
1. Application: By floated coat of adhesive to dry background in areas of about 1 m². Comb surface.
2. Tiling: Apply thin even coat of adhesive to backs of dry tiles. Fill any ribbed, deep keyed or button 

profiles. Press tiles firmly onto float coat.
3. Finished adhesive thickness: 3 mm or within the range allowed by the adhesive manufacturer.

652 Adhesive bed – buttering method (walls) 
1. Tiling: Apply even coat of adhesive to backs of dry tiles. Fill any ribbed, deep keyed or button 

profiles.
2. Finished adhesive thickness: 3 mm or within the range allowed by the adhesive manufacturer.

690 Cement:sand mortar bedding (walls) 
1. Preparation: Dampen background.
2. Application: By floated coat to background: 1:3-4 cement:sand mortar bedding. 

2.1. Thickness (maximum): 10 mm.
2.2. Finish: Equivalent to wood float. Before tiling allow to stiffen slightly.

3. Tiling: Without delay, apply 2 mm-thick coat of 1:1 cement:fine sand mortar to backs of tiles, filling 
keys. Press tiles firmly onto float coat. Tap firmly into position

720 Cement:sand mortar bedding (floors) 
1. Mortar bedding mix: 1:3-4 cement:sand. 

1.1. Consistency: Stiff plastic.
2. Laying: Lay suitably small working areas of screeded bed. Compact thoroughly to level. 

2.1. Finished bed thickness   
2.1.1. For tiles up to 10 mm thick: 10-15 mm.
2.1.2. For tiles greater than 10 mm thick: 15-20 mm.

3. Tiling: Within two hours and before bedding sets, evenly coat backs of tiles with adhesive. Press 
tiles firmly into position.

4. Finished adhesive thickness: Within range recommended by manufacturer.

730 Semi-dry cement:sand bedding (floors) 
1. Mortar bedding mix: 1:3.5-4 cement:sand. 

1.1. Water content: A film of water must not form on surface of bed when fully compacted.
2. Preparation: Dampen base.
3. Laying: Lay suitably small working areas of screeded bed. Compact thoroughly to level. 

3.1. Finished bed thickness (minimum): To be agreed with the Architect.
3.2. Finished bed thickness (maximum): To be agreed with the Architect.

4. Tiling: Within two hours and before bedding sets, evenly coat backs of tiles with neat cement 
slurry. Beat tiles firmly into position.

740 Cement:lime:sand mortar bedding (floors) 
1. Mortar bedding mix: 1:1: 5-6 cement:lime:sand. 

1.1. Cement: Portland to BS EN 197-1, type CEM I/42.5.
1.2. Lime:sand (coarse stuff): Ready-mixed to BS EN 998-2.
1.3. Sand: Fine aggregate to BS EN 13139.
1.4. Grading designation: 0/4 (MP) category 1 fines and between 20%-66% passing a 0.5 sieve.
1.5. Consistency: Stiff plastic. A film of water must not form on surface of bed when fully 

compacted.
2. Preparation: Dampen base.
3. Laying: Lay and compact bed to level. 

3.1. Finished bed thickness (minimum): 15 mm.
3.2. Finished bed thickness (maximum): 50 mm.

4. Tiling: Tamp tiles firmly into position.

750 Natural hydraulic lime mortar bedding (floors)

1.1. Mix: 2:5 NHL5 hydraulic lime:sharp well graded sand.
1.2. Sand: Fine aggregate to BS EN 13139, Annex B.
1.3. Consistency: Stiff plastic. A film of water must not form on the surface of bed when fully 

compacted.

Preparation
400 Preparation generally 
1. Standard: In accordance with BS 6150, BS 8000: Part 12, BS EN ISO 2808
2. Refer to any pre-existing CDM Health and Safety File.
3. Refer to CDM Construction Phase Plan where applicable.
4. Suspected existing hazardous materials: Prepare risk assessments and method statements 

covering operations, disposal of waste, containment and reoccupation, and obtain approval before 
commencing work.

5. Preparation materials: Types recommended by their manufacturers and the coating manufacturer 
for the situation and surfaces being prepared.

6. Substrates: Sufficiently dry in depth to suit coating.
7. Efflorescence salts: Remove.
8. Dirt, grease and oil: Remove. Give notice if contamination of surfaces/ substrates has occurred.
9. Surface irregularities: Remove.
10. Joints, cracks, holes and other depressions: Fill flush with surface, to provide smooth finish.
11. Dust, particles and residues from preparation: Remove and dispose of safely.
12. Water based stoppers and fillers   

12.1. Apply before priming unless recommended otherwise by manufacturer.
12.2. If applied after priming: Patch prime.

13. Oil based stoppers and fillers: Apply after priming.
14. Doors, opening windows and other moving parts   

14.1. Ease, if necessary, before coating.
14.2. Prime resulting bare areas.

440 Previously coated surfaces generally 
1. Preparation: In accordance with BS 6150, clause 11.5.
2. Contaminated or hazardous surfaces: Give notice of:

Client: GPE

2.1. Coatings suspected of containing lead.
2.2. Substrates suspected of containing asbestos or other hazardous materials.
2.3. Significant rot, corrosion or other degradation of substrates.

3. Suspected existing hazardous materials: Prepare risk assessments and method statements 
covering operations, disposal of waste, containment and reoccupation, and obtain approval before 
commencing work.

4. Removing coatings: Do not damage substrate and adjacent surfaces or adversely affect 
subsequent coatings.

5. Loose, flaking or otherwise defective areas: Carefully remove to a firm edge.
6. Alkali affected coatings: Completely remove.
7. Retained coatings   

7.1. Thoroughly clean to remove dirt, grease and contaminants.
7.2. Gloss-coated surfaces: Provide key.

8. Partly removed coatings   
8.1. Additional preparatory coats: Apply to restore original coating thicknesses.
8.2. Junctions: Provide flush surface.

9. Completely stripped surfaces: Prepare as for uncoated surfaces.

451 Previously coated surfaces – blast cleaning 
1. Operatives   

1.1. Trained/ experienced in blast cleaning.
1.2. Submit evidence of training/ experience on request.

2. Dust and nuisance: Minimize.

456 Previously coated surfaces – burning off 
1. Risk assessment and method statement: Prepare, and obtain approval before commencing work.
2. Adjacent areas: Protect from excessive heat and falling scrapings.
3. Exposed resinous areas and knots: Apply two coats of knotting.
4. Removed coatings: Dispose of safely.

560 Uncoated concrete 
1. Release agents: Remove.

570 Uncoated masonry/ Rendering 
1. Loose and flaking material: remove.

580 Uncoated plaster 
1. Nibs, trowel marks and plaster splashes: Scrape off.
2. Overtrowelled ‘polished’ areas: Key lightly.

622 Organic growths 
1. Dead and loose growths and infected coatings: Scrape off and remove from site.
2. Treatment biocide: Apply appropriate solution to growth areas and surrounding surfaces.
3. Residual effect biocide: Apply appropriate solution to inhibit re-establishment of growths.

1. General: To junctions of walls and ceilings with architraves, skirtings and other trims.

Application
711 Coating generally 
1. Application standard: In accordance with BS 6150, clause 9.
2. Conditions: Maintain suitable temperature, humidity and air quality during application and drying.
3. Surfaces: Clean and dry at time of application.
4. Thinning and intermixing of coatings: Not permitted unless recommended by manufacturer and 

agreed with the Architect.
5. Overpainting: Do not paint over intumescent strips or silicone mastics.
6. Priming coats   

6.1. Thickness: To suit surface porosity.
6.2. Application: As soon as possible on same day as preparation is completed.

7. Finish   
7.1. Even, smooth and of uniform colour.
7.2. Free from brush marks, sags, runs and other defects.
7.3. Cut in neatly.

8. Doors, opening windows and other moving parts: Ease before coating and between coats.
9. Application:: Material from a single batch, providing an even finish, free from any defects. 
10. Thickness:: Paint thickness must always be consistent and checked with a wet film thickness 

wheel or comb gauge. 
11. Drying:: Must never be accelerated through artificial means such as heaters or dehumidifiers. 
12. Final Coat:: Applied as a single coat and once dried it shall be viewed from 2 metres to determine 

colour consistency and quality. 
13. Inspection: : Conducted on-site by appropriately qualified person to inspect the work/ quality 

control documentation/ test results.

740 Concealed metal surfaces 
1. General: Apply additional coatings to surfaces that will be concealed when component is fixed in 

place.

800 Glazing 
1. Etched, sand blasted and ground glass: Treat or mask edges before coating to protect from 

contamination by oily constituents of coating materials.

M61
Intumescent coatings for fire protection of steelwork

100 Design Compliance
