110 FRP-101 Internal 90 Minute Intumescent Paint System - Basic 
1. Description: Factory-applied intumescent paint system to internal non-visible elements, to provide 

90 minutes fire resistance, with acrylic finish topsealer coat or suitable topsealer coat as 
recommended by the system manufacturer.

2. Location: Internal non-visible elements, refer to the Design Drawings.
3. Fire Resistance: 90 Minutes to comply with BS EN 13501-2 to 4, suitable for all steel sizes. 

3.1. To suit the service conditions, in accordance with the Fire Strategy Report and Drawings.
4. Surface: Shot blasted internal non-visible steelwork, as described within the Structural Engineer’s 

Steelwork Specification.
5. Surfaces shall be coated overall by airless spray as follows (other elements to be included as 

required): 
5.1. Primer/ base coat: In accordance with the corrosion protection category requirements, as 

described within the Structural Engineer’s Steelwork Specification. 
5.1.1. Maximum Volatile Organic Compound (VOC): 200gms/ kg.
5.1.2. Volume solids: 67%.

5.2. Intumescent coat: Suitable thickness of fire protection DFT to achieve the specified fire 
resistance. 

5.2.1. Liaise with the manufacturer and produce a schedule of thicknesses for coatings for 
individual steel sections based on the structural steel information and as-built site 
verification for acceptance by the Employer.

5.3. Sealer coat: Sealer coat DFT as per manufacturer's recommendations.
6. Finish: Basic, as indicated on the Design Drawings and described herein.
7. Colour: To be agreed with the Architect.

Performance and general requirements
190 Architectural and functional requirements 
1. General: 

1.1. Coatings to be applied by a licensed specialist and shall be in accordance with BS EN ISO 
4618.

1.2. The coatings of the entire system shall be covered by a single source warranty, approval 
shall be obtained from the manufacturer for all materials to be used.

1.3. Self adhesive labels shall be applied to the steel in accepted locations, providing the
following information: 

1.3.1. The manufacturer of the coating system.
1.3.2. Warning regarding damage to and removal of coatings.
1.3.3. Requirements regarding maintenance and overcoating.

2. Intumescent coatings: 
2.1. Coatings shall be compatible with each other, substrates and other products and methods 

associated with the coating system/ application of the system including the preparation 
thereof.

2.2. Intumescent coatings shall be applied to all steelwork including all steel interfacing elements 
as indicated on the Design Drawings, Working Drawings and Structural Engineer's 
documentation or as required for fire protection.

2.3. Substrates shall be suitably prepared.
2.4. The intumescent coating system shall generally comprise the following elements, in 

accordance with the manufacturer's recommendations to achieve the requirements of the 
Specification, other elements shall be included as required: 

2.4.1. Particular preparatory process and primer/ basecoat(s).
2.4.2. Intumescent coat(s).
2.4.3. Sealer coat(s).

2.5. The intumescent coating system shall be compatible with the required levels of corrosion 
protection for the specified environmental category in accordance with BS EN ISO 12944: 
Part 2, refer to the Structural Engineer's documentation.

2.6. Determine the thicknesses of the various coatings required for the systems to achieve
the requirements of the Specification, refer to Clause 407.

2.7. Apply a compatible prefabrication primer as recommended by the manufacturer.
2.8. Finish appearance: Basic and High Decorative, as Clauses 440 and 460.

195 Source of materials

196 Properties of coatings 
1. Coatings to have anti-mould properties.
2. Coatings to be lead free.
3. Volatile Organic Compound (VOC) levels shall be controlled in accordance with currentlegislation. 
4. The percentage by volume of solids to be in accordance with the requirements of the Specification.
5. Xylene based protective coatings shall not be permitted for site application without prior 

demonstration to the Employer that maximum safety precautions have been taken in strict 
adherence to the coating manufacturer's Control of Substances Hazardous to Health (COSHH) 
information and consequential recommendations.

197 Visual requirements 
1. Visual requirements shall be based on agreed submitted samples.
2. Specular gloss levels specified in accordance with BS EN ISO 2813.
3. Adhesion failure, excess fading, non-uniformity of colour, cracking, peeling, pitting or

other visual defects shall be defined by the descriptions contained in BS 2015 and BS EN ISO 
4618.
None of the above defects shall be acceptable.

4. The work shall be viewed with normal eyesight from a distance of 2m for colour consistency and 
excessive fading and 1m for cracking, pitting and other defects.

198 Scratch resistance 
1. Coatings to comply with the minimum requirements of BS EN ISO 1518: Part 1.

200 Structural performance 
1. The works shall be capable of accommodating loads imposed upon the systems to which

they are applied without any reduction in the specified performance.
2. Thermal Movement: 

2.1. Allow for local thermal movements exerted due to climatic conditions.
2.2. The works shall accommodate changes in dimension and shape of the components

to which application is to be made from changes in service temperatures and from
differential surface temperatures without any reduction in the specified performance.
The design shall cater for all temporary and permanent conditions envisaged for the
works.

1.1. Due to changes in the moisture content of components, resulting from variations in the

276 Testing - Coating Thickness 
1. Perform the following tests and inspections using qualified personnel and equipment unless

1.1. Wet film thickness measurements shall be taken as necessary during application, using

a wet comb or similar, to ensure that the required thickness of the various coatings is
achieved.

1.2. Dry film thickness (DFT) measurement shall be taken as required in accordance with
the methods described in BS EN ISO 2808, to ensure that the work has achieved the
required thickness.

280 Site work 
1. The workmanship shall generally be in accordance with BS 8202: Part 2 and the relevant

and applicable parts of BS 8000 and carried out in accordance with the manufacturer's
recommendations.

2. Due allowance shall be made for the sequencing of the whole works and all interfaces. The
sequence shall ensure that surfaces inaccessible after assembly receive the full specified 
treatment and coating system.

3. The works to be undertaken under cover and in properly lit, ventilated and heated conditions, as 
appropriate.

4. Provided manufacturer information shall be strictly adhered to. If the steel fabricator does not have 
the capability of applying the complete specified system at the works then the Contractor shall 
subcontract the painting programme to a contractor recommended by the system manufacturer.

5. All paints and other products shall be marked or labelled and stored in such a way that
identification of product and batch numbers is possible at all times. Ensure, whenever possible, 
that coating materials are from one manufacturing batch. Where more than one batch is to be 
used, keep separate, allocate to distinct parts or areas of the work, and inform the Employer 
accordingly.

281 Transport, storage and handling 
1. During transportation, separators shall be provided to prevent steel-to-steel contact and shall be 

adequate for intended use. Separators used to reduce transit damage may be utilised for the same 
purpose in Site storage. Suitably-sized timbers shall be used on which steel may be laid out with 
minimum damage to the intumescent coating.

Make Limited M61
30-08-2024 Intumescent coatings for fire protection of steelwork

Page 239 of 378



01061 - Jermyn Street Piccadilly – Detailed Architectural Specification - Detailed Revisions
Client: GPE

2. During transportation and storage it is essential that the coatings be protected from the possible 
effects of ponded water.

3. Where steelwork has undergone off-site factory paint or other finishing processes, minimise 
chafing, chipping and other damage to the coated components during transport and handling.
Where steelwork has been designed without lifting holes or lugs utilise special lashings, lifting 
harnesses nylon slings and webbing, rubber protective chains and chocks or other suitable 
methods/ equipment to help spread the loads over a larger surface area.

290 Off-site-coated steel 
1. Handling and erection: Use methods and devices designed to minimize damage to intumescent 

coatings.
2. Damaged areas of coating: Reinstate in accordance with coating manufacturer's 

recommendations.

Preparation of surfaces
300 General 
1. Apply coatings to suitably dry, clean, dust free, surfaces in dry atmospheric conditions and after 

any previous coats have cured adequately.
2. Evenly apply coatings to give a smooth finish of uniform thickness and colour, free from brush 

marks, nibs, sags, runs and other defects.
3. Where required, brush apply an additional stripe coat to the edges, welds, corners of flanges, bolt 

heads, nuts and any areas difficult to coat.
4. Keep all surfaces clean and free from dust during coating and drying. Adequately protect 

completed work from damage.
5. Allow an interval of at least the period recommended by the manufacturer between successive 

coats of the system. Ensure that an adequate drying/ curing period for each coat has elapsed 
before handling.

6. Multiple coats of the same material shall be of a different tint to ensure that each coat provides 
complete coverage. Consideration shall be given to the correct choice of colour for the undercoat 
such that it complements the finished topcoat colour and avoids any colour variation showing 
through the topcoat.

301 Condition suitability 
1. Do not apply coatings: 

1.1. In conditions not compliant with the safety risk assessments and method statements.
1.2. Surfaces affected by frost or moisture.
1.3. When heat is likely to cause blistering or wrinkling.
1.4. Unless the steel temperature is at least 3°C above the dew point with conditions

stable or improving, or as otherwise recommended by the coating manufacturer.
1.5. Unless the relative humidity is below 85% or as otherwise recommended by the

coating manufacturer.
2. The works shall be applied in conditions that are safe for the operatives and other

personnel coming into contact with the works during and after application.
3. All necessary precautions shall be taken during and after application, including restrictions on 

working hours, providing temporary protection and allowing extra drying time, to ensure that 
coatings are not adversely affected by atmospheric and environmental conditions.

315 New steel – blast-cleaning 
1. Preparation: Remove oil, grease and contaminants.
2. Blast-cleaning   
Make Limited M61
30-08-2024 Intumescent coatings for fire protection of steelwork

Page 240 of 378



01061 - Jermyn Street Piccadilly – Detailed Architectural Specification - Detailed Revisions
Client: GPE

2.1. Atmospheric condition: Dry.
2.2. Abrasive: Suitable type and size, free from fines, moisture and oil.
2.3. Finish: To BS EN ISO 8501-1, preparation grade SA2½, with an average profile of 

approximately 75 micrometres.
2.4. Abrasive residues and moisture: Remove.

3. Primer: Apply as soon as possible after cleaning and before gingering or blackening appears.

320 Existing steel – blast-cleaning 
1. Preparation: Remove oil, grease and contaminants.
2. Blast-cleaning: Remove existing coatings and rust. 

2.1. Atmospheric condition: Dry.
2.2. Abrasive: Suitable type and size, free from fines, moisture and oil.
2.3. Finish: To BS EN ISO 8501-1, preparation grade SA2½, with an average profile of 

approximately 75 micrometres.
2.4. Abrasive residues and moisture: Remove.

3. Primer: Apply as soon as possible after cleaning and before gingering or blackening appears.

330 Existing steel – manual cleaning 
1. Preparation: Remove oil, grease and contaminants.
2. Loose or unsound coatings: Remove to a firm edge.
3. Finish: To BS EN ISO 8501-1, preparation grade St2. Leave a clean but unpolished dry surface.
4. Primer: Apply as soon as possible after cleaning and before gingering or blackening appears. 

Remove coating edges that lift as a result of priming, and reprime.

340 Existing steel – preparation for overcoating 
1. Preparation: Remove oil, grease and contaminants.
2. Loose or unsound coatings: Remove to a firm edge.
3. Exposed steel finish: Manually clean to BS EN ISO 8501-1, grade St2. Leave a clean but 

unpolished dry surface.
4. Existing coatings finish: Abrade to give a good key. Leave a clean, dry surface.
5. Primer: Apply one brush coat to bare steel areas. Remove coating edges that lift as a result of 

priming, and reprime.

Application of castings
400 Intumescent casting installation 
1. Assembly: Adhesive fix casting sections tight to steel substrate.
2. Filling: Apply intumescent filler to all joints and deformations to produce a smooth and uniform 

finish.

401 Intumescent casting thickness 
1. Required thickness: Determine for every steel member to give specified period of fire resistance. 

Use intumescent casting manufacturer's current published loading tables. 
1.1. Special sections and partial fire exposure conditions: Obtain required thickness in writing 

from manufacturer.
2. Schedule and drawings: Submit at least two weeks before starting work.

2.1. Schedule content: Member sizes, weights/ thicknesses, loading conditions, etc. showing, for 
each variant, the exposed perimeter/ sectional area (Hp/A) ratio and required casting 
thickness.

2.2. Drawing content: Steelwork drawings marked in colour to show required thickness for each 
member.

Application of coatings
405 Initial coats 
1. Preparation coatings used shall be of the types recommended by the coating manufacturer for the 

condition and surfaces being prepared to receive new finishes.

406 Intumescent and finishing coats 
1. Once applied the finish shall not in any way slump, flow, crack, flake, split, sag, pit, bubble, blister, 

float, effloresce, craze, shrink, break, wrinkle, crinkle, yellow, chalk, fade, discolour, powder, stain, 
bleed or lose its finish or gloss in any way. 

2. No variation of final surface finish after application.
3. Unsatisfactory paintwork shall be made good with additional coats of material at the Contractor's 

own expense, to the acceptance of the Employer.

407 Film thickness 
1. Check the thickness of each coat during application using a wet film thickness wheel or comb 

gauge in accordance with BS EN ISO 2808.
2. After each coat has dried, measure the total accumulated dry film thickness in accordance with the 

methods described in BS EN ISO 2808, the number and position of measurements to be as 
directed by the Employer. Carry out all measurements in the presence of the Employer, unless 
otherwise directed.

3. If at any stage the accumulated dry film thickness is deficient, the Employer may require 
application of additional coats, at no extra cost.

4. The dry film thickness of the topcoat shall be sufficient to give an even, solid, opaque appearance, 
irrespective of the number of applied topcoats necessary to achieve cosmetic obliteration of the 
undercoat. The full specified topcoat dry film thickness shall be maintained, notwithstanding any 
greater than specified undercoat dry film thickness.

408 Intumescent coating dry film thickness (DFT) 
1. Required dft: Determine for every steel member to give specified period of fire resistance. Use 

intumescent coating manufacturer's current published loading tables. 
1.1. Special sections and partial fire exposure conditions: Obtain required DFT in writing from 

manufacturer.
2. Schedule and drawings: Submit at least two weeks before starting work. 

2.1. Schedule content: Member sizes, weights/ thicknesses, loading conditions, etc. showing, for 
each variant, the exposed perimeter/ sectional area (Hp/A) ratio and required DFT.

2.2. Drawing content: Steelwork drawings marked in colour to show required DFT for each 
member.

409 Measurement of intumescent DFT 
1. Primer dft: Determine average DFT (for deduction from total DFT after application of intumescent).
2. Intumescent dft: Determine at: 

2.1. 500 mm centres along each coated plane of universal sections (8 planes), and rectangular 
hollow sections (4 planes).

2.2. 125 mm centres along coated circular hollow sections, spread evenly around circumference.
Make Limited M61
30-08-2024 Intumescent coatings for fire protection of steelwork

Page 242 of 378



01061 - Jermyn Street Piccadilly – Detailed Architectural Specification - Detailed Revisions
Client: GPE

3. Acceptance standard   
3.1. Average intumescent dft: Not less than required DFT (exclusive of primer and top sealer).
3.2. Local intumescent dft: Not less than 80% of required dft. Areas greater than 100 mm 

equivalent diameter with a dft of less than 80% of required dft must be brought up to 
thickness.

420 Making good 
1. Splashes resulting from work carried out on Site shall be cleared from all surfaces.
2. Any coated area mechanically damaged in transit or at site is to be hand or power tool cleaned to 

St2 standard (BS EN ISO 8501: Part 1). Touch up bare areas to meet the specified requirements. 
Adhere to the printed recommendations of the coating system manufacturer regarding appropriate 
method of application.

3. Any remedial work shall be in accordance with the manufacturer's recommendations and to match 
the original finish. Samples of remedial work shall be submitted to the Employer for acceptance 
prior to commencement of work.

4. Early degrading of coatings by blistering, peeling, flaking, cracking or lack of adhesion shall be 
made good by complete removal, re-preparation and reapplication of all coats, as instructed.

5. Inadequate dry film thickness or surface defects due to adverse weather shall, depending on the 
type of intumescent coating system, be remedied by rubbing down and applying further coat(s), as 
instructed.

6. Mechanical damage to coatings shall be made good by local cutting back of coatings, re-
preparation and reapplication of all coats to leave a neat, continuous and flat finish.

7. Where damage to coatings or subsequent surface preparation has exposed bare metal, it shall be 
thoroughly cleaned and primed within two hours.

440 Basic finish 
1. Definition: Reasonably smooth and even. Orange peel, other texture, minor runs and similar minor 

defects are acceptable.

450 Normal decorative finish 
1. Definition: Good standard of cosmetic finish generally, when viewed from a distance of 5m or 

more. Minor orange peel or other texture is acceptable.

460 High decorative finish 
1. Definition: High standard of evenness, smoothness and gloss when viewed from a minimum 

distance of 2m.

490 Top sealer coat 
1. Application: To achieve dft recommended by manufacturer and to give an even, solid, opaque 

appearance, free from runs, sags and other visual defects.

520 Completion of off-site-coated steel 
1. Uncovered areas, including fixings: Following erection of steelwork, apply intumescent coating 

locally.
2. Unscheduled additional connections to erected steelwork: Remove and reinstate intumescent 

coating locally.

550 Tolerances 
1. Tolerances shall be within the limits recommended by the respective manufacturer to suit the 

service conditions.

610 Installation generally 
1. Assembly and fixing: Surfaces designed to falls to drain as intended.
2. Fasteners: Nonferrous or stainless steel.
3. Supply and discharge pipework: Fix before appliances.
4. Fixing: Fix appliances securely to structure. Do not support on pipework. Once fixed, all elements 

to be stable, firmly fixed and free from leaks including when in use. 
5. Jointing and bedding compounds: Recommended by manufacturers of appliances, accessories 

and pipes being jointed or bedded.
6. Appliances: Do not use. Do not stand on appliances.
7. On completion: Components and accessories working correctly with no leaks.
8. Labels and stickers: Remove.
9. Compliance: BS 3402 - Specification for quality of vitreous china sanitary appliances.

BS EN 33 - WC pans and WC suites. 
BS EN 997 - WC pans and WC suites with integral trap.
BS EN 13407 - Wall-hung urinals. 
BS 1206 - Specification for fireclay sinks.
BS EN 14688 - Sanitary appliances. 
BS EN 31 - Wash basins. BS EN 14055 - WC and urinal flushing cisterns.
BS EN 200 - Sanitary tapware.
BS EN ISO 1456 - Metallic and other inorganic coatings.
BS EN 14516 - Baths for domestic purposes.
BS EN 198 - Sanitary appliances.BS EN 14428 - Shower enclosures. 
BS EN 14527 - Shower trays for domestic purposes.

710 Electrical installation 
1. General: Install, test and commission electrical work forming part of the access equipment system 

up to and including wiring of isolating switch or switches provided by the electrical contractor, all in 
accordance with BS 7671.

2. Components/ Fasteners: Use waterproof components and corrosion resisting fasteners 
throughout. Avoid contact between dissimilar metals.

3. Control system: Designed so that an electrical supply failure, or the failure or malfunction of any 
electrical component, will cause the system to fail to safety.

4. Failsafe provision: In the event of power failure the system must be capable of delivering the 
working platform to a position of safety either by hand winding or by a controlled gravity descent 
device.

5. Isolating switches: Clearly labelled to identify purpose. Labels to display an emergency contact

640 Installing boarding 
1. Position of boarding: Within opening.
2. Framing: Provide framing to all edges of boarding.
3. Bedding: Bed boarding on fire-resisting adhesive/ sealant.
4. Multiple board layers: Stagger joints between layers. 

4.1. Joints: Seal with board adhesive.
5. Fixing: Submit proposals.
6. Other requirements: Finish joint sealant flush with boards.

650 Installing flexible intumescent gap sealer 
1. Fitting of strips: Compress strips and fit into gap so that, as they decompress, the strips wedge 

themselves in the void.
2. Shrink wrapping: Do not remove.
3. Joints   

3.1. Ends of strips: Fit intumescent 'end piece' at both ends of run of fire stop laminate.
3.2. Joints between strips: Fit two intumescent 'end pieces' at each butt joint.

660 Applying intumescent foam 
1. New joints: Remove builders' debris, mortar droppings, grease, and other contaminants.

Make Limited P12
30-08-2024 Fire-stopping systems

Page 300 of 378



01061 - Jermyn Street Piccadilly – Detailed Architectural Specification - Detailed Revisions
Client: GPE

2. Old joints: Clean and remove existing sealant from each joint.
3. Priming: Lightly moisten substrate with water.
4. Application: Fill joint to approximately half its depth, and allow foam to expand to face of joint.
5. Trimming: Trim excess foam to give a neat, flush appearance.

670 Applying intumescent mortar 
1. Sequence: Install mortar after services are permanently installed.
2. Loose dust and combustible materials: Remove from the opening.
3. Shuttering: Install suitable shuttering panels to the faces of the opening.
4. Temperature: Do not apply mortar when it could be damaged by frost.
5. Mortar cure: Do not disturb mortar before final set has taken place.
6. Shuttering: Remove after mortar has cured.

680 Installing intumescent pillows 
1. Number of pillows (per m² of opening): Number necessary to achieve fire resistance.
2. Orientation of bags: Parallel to plane of construction element containing opening.

690 Applying intumescent putty 
1. Sequence: Install putty after services are permanently installed.
2. Loose dust and combustible materials: Do not disturb putty before final set has taken place.

710 Installing mineral wool batts 
1. Installing batts: Fit tight into void between the penetrating services and the surrounding 

construction to form a solid barrier. 
1.1. Brackets: Impale batts on proprietary pressed steel brackets at 500 mm maximum centres 

and not greater than 250 mm from ends of batts.
2. Face of batts: Flush with the surface of wall, floor or soffit.
3. Joints between batts: Butt joints, seal with fire-resisting sealant.
4. Gaps between services and barrier: Seal with fire-resisting sealant.

730 Fixing pipe collars 
1. Collar fixing: Submit proposals.
2. Gap around collar: Submit proposals.
3. Length of wraps: Project 50 mm from each side of the element.

740 Inserting sealant backing material 
1. Preparation: Removed debris from service penetration.
2. Installation: Insert joint filler to full depth of joint leaving sufficient depth to apply sealant.

745 Applying sealants generally 
1. Application: As section Z22.

750 Applying capping sealant 
1. Preparation: Submit proposals.
2. Priming: Submit proposals.
3. Depth of sealant: Submit proposals.
4. Temperature: Do not apply water-based sealants when they could be damaged by frost.

205 Performance Generally 
1.  Achieve a slip resistance value of 36PTV in both dry and wet conditions when carrying out tests 

using a TRL Pendulum Test. 
2. Achieve a minima value of 20 μm RZ when carrying out tests with a surface roughness meter. 
3. Lift entrances (car and landing doors) to be fire rated. 
4. Lift finishes and components to remain stable and in place with no distortion, reduction in 

performance, deformity or change in appearance during operation or use. 
5. Upon completion and when in use there shall be no rattling, creaking or shaking of any component 

or finishes system within the lift car. 
6. Individual elements of the lift car such as handrail, ceiling panels, control boards and panelling are 

to be removable when in place for maintenance and replacement purposes.

620 Workmanship generally 
1. Lifts to be manufactured at a specialist factory in full compliance with QA/ QC requirements and 

within tolerances shown on the Working Drawings.
2. Carry out and survey of lift shafts on-Site prior to commencing manufacture or ensure that lift 

shafts are built to the required size within stated tolerances.
3. Ensure adequate space is allowed for controls and services in so far as they affect the lift finishes.
4. All components to be flawless and accurately manufactured such that when installed they are a 

perfect fit, are square, align and operate efficiently.
5. Preform all joints, mitres, etc. Joints to align between horizontal and vertical linings as shown on 

the Working Drawings.
6. Internal linings to be 1:1000 flatness criteria unless agreed otherwise.
7. All fixings to be hidden (non-visible).
8. Panels to be manufactured ±2mm in all directions with maximum ±1mm twist.
9. Shadow gaps/ plane offset not to exceed ±1mm.

Z01
Various Materials
To be read with preliminaries/ general conditions.
100 Cement/ Aggregate/ Water/ Sand 
1. Standards: comply with 

1.1.
• BS 197 - Cement.
• BS EN 1008 - Water for concrete.
• BS EN 12620 - Aggregates for concrete.
• BS EN 13055 - Lightweight aggregates.
• BS EN 13139 - Aggregates for mortar.
• PD 6682-3 - Aggregate guidance related to BS EN 13139.
• PD 6682 - Lightweight aggregate related to concrete, mortar and grout.
• GRCA recommendations.

2. Materials 
2.1. Sand: 

• Minimise organic impurities.
• Restrict the use of deleterious reactive materials containing alkalis in cement.
• Use coarse aggregate or dust/ salt/ silt free natural sand.
• Less than 10% of material to be able to pass through a 150 micron sieve.
• Maintain +/- 20% variation in fineness modulus for facing mix design.
• Remove all soluble material.
• Carefully control mix variables.

2.2. Water: To remain clean, fresh, potable and impurity free from a known and reliable source.

200 Plasterboard 
1. Standards: Comply with:  

1.1.
• BS 8000-8 - Code of Practice for plasterboard and partitions.
• BS 8212 - Plasterboard dry lining.
• BS EN 520 - Gypsum plasterboard.
• GPDA (Gypsum Products Development Association) recommendations.
• FIS (Finishes and Interiors Sector) recommendations.

2. Materials: 
2.1. Quality: 

• To be high performance type of 1500kg/ ￿ density when used in high humidity/ 
damp areas and/ or when walls are to receive tiling, linings and the like.

• In areas likely to be wet or very damp use moisture resistant plasterboard.
• Use Type A/ H/ E and P where standard sheets are required.
• Use Type I in high density areas such as cores where glass fibre is required as 

reinforcement.
• Metal foil backed sheets are not acceptable as a water/ vapour barrier.
• Before applying wet render of plaster to moisture resistant boards without suitable 

pre-treatment with an appropriate bonding agent. Such treatment to be agreed in 
advance.

• All boards to be new.
Make Limited Z01
30-08-2024 Various Materials

Page 327 of 378



01061 - Jermyn Street Piccadilly – Detailed Architectural Specification - Detailed Revisions
Client: GPE

3. Installation: 
3.1. Workmanship:

• Multiple layer boarding to be staggered by 600mm.
• Boards to be undamaged, clean and dry with no sign of having been wet prior to 

installation.
• Fix to suitably prepared and stable framework/ substrate.
• Only install in dry interior areas.
• Joints to be tight butts without any lipping, imperfect edges or indentations.
• Cuts to be straight and neat.
• Cut edges to be lightly sanded, sealed and smooth forming a tight continuous fit.
• Avoid using cut boards at perimeter conditions or at edges of penetrations.
• Use only recommended and appropriate fixings installed strictly in accordance with 

the manufacturer’s recommendations.
• Apply fixings at appropriate centres commencing in the middle of each board.
• Final surfaces to be smooth and flat without indentations caused by fixings.
• Taper edge joints and butt each sheet with a minimal gap of no more than 2mm.
• Stagger joints either side of the partition.
• Install appropriate flush beading/ reinforcement at edges, openings, perimeters, 

interfaces, stress points, etc. where necessary.
• Tape and fill all joints/ gaps to provide a smooth, flat and continuous surface finish 

using 100% secured tape and apply plaster skim coat of appropriate thickness.

2. Materials: 

2.1. General: Board grade to be sufficiently and suitably robust, sound, of suitable class/ 
thickness and moisture resistant to suit prevailing conditions and use.

2.2. Fibreboard: Only use bitumen impregnated fibreboard with cavity barriers.  
2.3. Laminated faced panels: 

• Core substrate to be of suitable type and quality of either:
o Plywood.

o Grade P5 particleboard.

o MDF.

o Blockboard.

o Laminboard.

• Control moisture content to suit prevailing environmental conditions and core 
material.

• Bond sheets in presses.
• Fully condition sheets prior to lamination.
• Oversize holes to compensate for shrinkage of the laminate.
• Final board thickness (including laminate) to provide:

o Sufficient structural strength.

o Impact resistance.

o Stability.

• Laminated panels to be balanced to stop warping, bowing, etc.
• Allow a minimum of 36 hours atmospheric conditioning prior to commencement of 

high pressure laminate bonding.
• All edges to be smooth cut and match adjoining panels/ surfaces.

Make Limited Z01
30-08-2024 Various Materials

Page 329 of 378



01061 - Jermyn Street Piccadilly – Detailed Architectural Specification - Detailed Revisions
Client: GPE

• Apply laminate to edges first followed by main surfaces.
• Edge and top joints to be chamfered at 45°.
• Black lines at edges are not acceptable.
• Final finish to be from from bowing/ twisting/ chips/ pimpling/ scratches/ cracks/ 

surface contamination, debris and marks/ depressions/ stains/ colour, texture and 
pattern irregularities.

• Edge conditions to be agreed as either:
o Hardwood lipping.

o Post formed edges.

o 3mm thick PVC in matching or agreed colour.

o Decorative laminate in matching or agreed colour, using radio frequency 
edgebanders.

o No edging.

3. Workmanship: 
3.1. Board fixing: 

• Run edges along supports.
• At 150mm c/c maximum to intermediate supports.
• At 100mm c/c maximum to edge boards.
• Inset 12mm from edge.

3.2. Plywood: 
• Take care storing on site to ensure protection from adverse environmental 

conditions with free airflow maintained at all times.
• Fix plywood:

o Long edges running at 90° to supports.

o End joints to be staggered in alignment with supports.

o 150mm c/c at perimeters/ edges.

o 300mm c/c at intermediate supports.

o Use 50mm x 3mm annular ring shank nails.

o Bond floor joints using PVA adhesive.

3.3. Chipboard: 
• Long edges running at 90° to supports.
• End joints to be staggered in alignment with supports.
• 300mm c/c at perimeters/ edges/ interim supports.
• Use 50mm x 3mm annular ring shank nails.
• Bond floor joints using PVA adhesive.
• Leave a 10mm gap around all perimeter edges for expansion.

3.4. Laminated panels: No lipping with flush joints and flat surfaces panel to panel in all 
directions.

400 Glass / Coatings 
1. Comply with:

• BS 952 - Glass for glazing.
• BS 5516-1 and 2 - Patent glazing.

• BS 6206 - Safety glass impact performance
• BS 6262 - Glazing in buildings.
• BS EN 572 - Lime silicate glass.

2. Materials 
2.1. General: 

• Use only float process to manufacture annealed glass.
• Glass to be clear, undistorted and free from reflection.
• Glass packaging to show place of manufacture.
• Glass to be sourced from a single source for each element of the Works.
• Only use kitemarked safety glass.
• Glass to be free from impurities.
• Glass/ glazing systems to be fully warranted and guaranteed to suit design and 

service life requirements.
• Glass/ glazing systems to be suitable for intended use, of an appropriate type/ 

quality, of adequate thickness and to an acceptable finish.
• Glass/ glazing systems to comply with all performance and visual intent 

requirements.
• Mirror glass is not acceptable unless specifically requested.

Make Limited Z01
30-08-2024 Various Materials

Page 331 of 378



01061 - Jermyn Street Piccadilly – Detailed Architectural Specification - Detailed Revisions
Client: GPE

• Glazing installations to be visually consistent and acceptable throughout, 
maintaining the appearance and quality of all accepted samples, prototypes and 
benchmarks.

• Visual acceptance to be determined by observation/ inspection from a distance of 
1000mm at multiple angles with both blue sky and cloudy backgrounds.

• The following are unacceptable:
o Visual distortion.

o Air bubbles.

o Imperfections of any kind.

o Smoke vanes.

• Glazing systems to be manufactured in specialist facilities using appropriate 
equipment to ensure pristine accuracy in terms of cutting, finishing, assembly, etc.

• Edges, joints, to be smooth, even, ground and arrised to provide a visually 
acceptable product.

• Assemblies to be installed safely into frames within the recommended/ agreed 
tolerances as shown on the Working Drawings.

• Panels to be designed to facilitate individual removal for maintenance/ removal 
purposes.

• Glazing systems are not to exert stress or external force on any component/ 
assembly in any condition that reduces its performance, visual appearance, shape 
or safety.

• Coatings and finishes to remain pristine.
• Carry out full stress analysis to confirm compliance.
• Wired glass to be minimum 3mm thick, using clear annealed sheet glass with 

1.3mm gauge georgian wire embedded during glass manufacture.
• Fire resistant safety glass to be Category B/ Class 2B2 minimum being clear and 

unwired incorporating fire rated beading.
2.2. Laminated glass: 

• To be carried out at a specialist facility.
• Bond a minimum of two layers of flat glass with a suitable clear interlayer between 

each panel.
• Accurately align each sheet to prevent overlapping at edges.
• No distortions are acceptable.
• Support bottom edges such that loads are supported evenly along the full length of 

each unit.
• Point loading is not acceptable.
• Fully seal all edges.

2.3. Heat strengthening glass: 
• To be carried out at a specialist facility.
• Fully control strengthening process and anisotropy to avoid reduction in 

performance, quality or visual intent.
• Edge dip not to exceed 0.25mm/300mm.
• Glass edge bowing to be less than 0.12mm/300mm .
• Flatness criteria to be 1mm in 1000mm in any direction and non accumulative over 

3000mm.
• Minimise roller wave distortion such that it is not visible/ detectable in any direction/ 

orientation.
• Small visible ripples may be accepted provided that they are horizontally consistent 

and evenly spaced over the whole panel.
2.4. Toughened glass: 

Make Limited Z01
30-08-2024 Various Materials

Page 332 of 378

Client: GPE

• Generally unacceptable unless agreed in advance in writing.
• Must be heat soaked, tested with full audit traceability and audit trail. 
• Provide full technical details and guarantee of performance, suitability and 

adherence to visual intent if toughening is proposed.
2.5. Curved glass: 

• To be carried out at a specialist facility.
• Maintain a consistent radius.
• Use specially prepared and appropriate types of glass. Demonstrate compliance 

through calculations and samples/ prototypes.
• Maintain performance requirements, visual intent and quality.
• Maintain tight tolerances to guarantee a consistent curve when multiple panels and 

connected. 
• Maintain perfect alignment at vertical and horizontal edges which are to be 

continuous with consistent joint widths.
• Maximum allowable deviation in curvature of a complete installation to be a 

maximum of ±4mm from the theoretical position.
2.6. Mirror glass: To be:

• New.
• Clean.
• Flat.
• Clear.
• Smooth.
• Free of defects, marks or blemishes.
• Completely undistorted.

2.7. Horizontal/ inclined glazing: 
• Use only suitably strengthened glass.
• Fix into a suitable and adequate support framing system which is structurally sound.
• Fixing/ restraining methods to guarantee a safe installation.
• Performance requirements and visual intent to be maintained.
• Glass to remain in place (i.e. no pieces of glass or support system will fall to the 

ground) should damage or breakage occur.
• System to be sufficiently sound to resist all live and dead loads to which it is 

subjected including accidental damage, maintenance, cleaning, etc. 
• Carry out impact tests to an energy level of 1200J minimum.

Fabrication
515 Fabrication generally 
1. Contact between dissimilar metals in components: Avoid.
2. Finished components: Rigid and free from distortion, cracks, burrs and sharp arrises. 

2.1. Moving parts: Free moving without binding.
3. Corner junctions of identical sections: Mitre.
4. Aluminium/ metal sheets: To be ridged fully welded/ sealed, tempered, and visually flat (1:1000 

over 3000mm in all directions), without distortion.
5. Corners: Corners to comprise purpose made components and not cut unless shown otherwise on 

the Working Drawings. Colour and finish to match adjoining elements.
6. Protection: Provide adequate protection at all times.
7. Insulation: Provide, where necessary, to negate drumming and other noise.
8. Joints: To be concealed, continuous and sealed.
9. Ends: Aluminium ends to be mill finished. Metal ends to be hot dipped galvanised.

520 Cold formed work 
1. Profiles: Accurate, with straight arrises.

525 Adhesive bonding 
1. Preparation of surfaces of metals to receive adhesives   

1.1. Degrease.
1.2. Abrade mechanically or chemically etch.
1.3. Prime: To suit adhesive.

2. Adhesive bond: Form under pressure.

527 Welding 
1. Description: Continuous and suitable.
2. Welding procedures   

2.1. Method and standard: Full penetration welds, flush ground where visible. Use jigs/ clamps, 
as necessary, to ensure a precise fit.

2.2. Welding Procedure Specification (WPS): Provide written procedure statement.
3. Preparation   

3.1. Joint preparation: Clean thoroughly.
3.2. Surfaces of materials that will be self-finished and visible in the completed work: protect 

from weld splatter, residue build up and splatter.
4. Jointing   

4.1. Joints: Fully bond parent and filler metal throughout with no inclusions, holes, porosity or 
cracks.

4.2. Dissimilar metals: Use appropriate material and techniques.
4.3. Strength requirements: Welds to achieve design loads.
4.4. Heat straightening: Only when agreed in advance.
4.5. Complex assemblies: Agree priority for welding members to minimize distortion caused by 

subsequent welds.
4.6. Tack welds: Use only for temporary attachment.
4.7. Jigs: Provide to support and restrain members during welding.
4.8. Filler plates: Obtain approval.

Make Limited Z11
30-08-2024 Purpose made metalwork

Page 349 of 378



01061 - Jermyn Street Piccadilly – Detailed Architectural Specification - Detailed Revisions
Client: GPE

4.9. Lap joints: Minimum 5 x metal thickness or 25 mm, whichever is greater.
4.10. Weld terminations: Clean and sound.
4.11. Workmanship: Visible holes, inclusions, ridges, cracks, undercuts or porosity are not 

acceptable.
4.12. Stainless steel welds:   

4.12.1. Grind to leave smooth and seamless surface.
4.12.2. Remove heat tints using light abrasives, wire brushing, pickling paste or other similar 

and acceptable method.
4.12.3. Comply with BS 499 and BS EN 101.
4.12.4. Minimise distortion during welding.
4.12.5. All welds, methods and consumables to achieve, as a minimum, the same level of 

corrosion resistance as the base material.

530 Stainless steel fabrication 
1. Guillotining or punching: Do not use for metal thicknesses greater that 10 mm.
2. Thermal cutting   

2.1. Carbonation in the heat affected zone: Remove, after cutting.
3. Bending   

3.1. Plates or bars: Cold bending radius not less than material thickness.
3.2. Tubes: Cold bending radius not less than 2 x tube diameter.

4. Welding: In addition to general welding requirements: 
4.1. Protect adjacent surfaces from weld spatter.
4.2. Pickle all welds before post fabrication treatments.

5. Protection: Provide protection to fabricated components during transit and on site.
6. Corrosion: Not acceptable.

610 Fixing generally 
1. Integrity of supported components: Select types, sizes, quantities and spacings of fixings,

2. Components, substrates, fixings and fasteners of dissimilar metals: Isolate with washers/ sleeves 

to avoid bimetallic corrosion.
3. Fixing to structure: Record all primary fixings to include size, type, spacing, embedment, precise 

location, edge dimensions, shimming, isolation techniques for dissimilar metals, locking washers, 
etc. and test results.

4. Bolts: After tightening, leave a minimum of one clear thread above each nut.
5. Appearance: Fixings to be in straight lines at regular centres.
6. Compliance: To Approved Document Part A, Section 2 of the Building Regulations.
7. Tools: Use purpose made tooling systems where appropriate and necessary to ensure consistent 

and adequate depths and perpendicular installation.
8. Screws:: Countersunk screw heads visible in the finished works require countersinks to be 

machined in order to receive them.

manufacturer.

4.3. In floors, allow 10mm gap at each edge, parallel to the lie of the boards.

3. Fixings in masonry work: Fully bed in mortar.

1. Penetration: Drive fully in without splitting or crushing timber.
2. Surfaces visible in completed work: Punch nail heads below wrot surfaces.
3. Nailed timber joints: Two nails per joint (minimum), opposed skew driven.

1. Finished level of countersunk screw heads   

1.1. Exposed: Flush with timber surface.

1.2. Concealed (holes filled or stopped): Sink minimum 2 mm below surface.

1. Finished level of countersunk screw heads: Minimum 6 mm below timber surface.

dampness.

section plastic washers.

700 Applying adhesives

adhesive.
2. Support and clamping during setting: Provide as necessary. Do not mark surfaces of or distort 

components being fixed.
3. Finished adhesive joints: Fully bonded. Free of surplus adhesive.
4. Conditions:: Do not use during unsuitable climatic/ environmental conditions, after recommended 

“use by” date/ open pot times.
5. Application::   

5.1. Use the appropriate primer which is left to dry before adhesive is applied.
5.2. Use recommended spreaders/ applicators/ tools to ensure adequate and even coverage.
5.3. Remove surplus adhesive before it dries.

Mortars
Cement gauged mortars
110 Cement gauged mortar mixes 
1. Specification: Proportions and additional requirements for mortar materials are specified 

elsewhere.
2. Standards:   

2.1. BS 1996-1 - Design of masonry structures. Reference Table NA2.
2.2. BS EN 998-1 - Render and plastering mortar.
2.3. BS EN 998-2 - Masonry mortar.

3. Appearance: No colour difference above and below DPC level or between different daily 
installations. 
3.1. BS 1996-1 - Design of masonry structures. Reference Table NA2.
3.2. BS EN 998-1 - Render and plastering mortar.
3.3. BS EN 998-2 - Masonry mortar.

4. Performance: Mix to meet performance requirements in all conditions and locations.
5. Workmanship:   

5.1. Mix thoroughly in a suitable forced action mechanical mixer to produce uniform/ consistent 
colour and workable consistency.

5.2. Do not use a free fall drum mixer.
5.3. Minimise water content whilst maintaining required consistency/ workability.
5.4. Batch by volume using dry sand.
5.5. Maintain a mix ratio of 1:3.5/ 4 cement to sand when mixing semi-dry mortar and ensure that 

it does not crumble or deform when squeezed or form a film of water on the surface.
5.6. Maintain a mix ratio of no less than 1:3.5/ 4 cement to sand when mixing a floating mortar 

bed and ensure that it does not crumble or deform when squeezed or form a film of water on 
the surface. Lay on a smooth and thoroughly cleaned base material with a minimum 500 
gauge polythene separating lay over insulation with minimum 100mm laps at joints. Mix to 
be semi-dry incorporating steel mesh as necessary.

6. Proprietary mortars: Ensure that allowance is made for selection/addition of project-specific 
aggregate such as may be required to obtain the visual acceptance of the Architect.

120 Sand for site made cement gauged masonry mortars 
1. Standard: To BS EN 13139.
2. Grading: 0/2 (FP or MP). 

2.1. Fines content where the proportion of sand in a mortar mix is specified as a range (e.g. 
1:1: 5-6): 

2.1.1. Lower proportion of sand: Use category 3 fines.
2.1.2. Higher proportion of sand: Use category 2 fines.

3. Sand for facework mortar: Maintain consistent colour and texture. Obtain from one source.
4. Marine sand: Do not use with sulphate resisting or super sulphated cement.

500 Testing 
1. Laboratory:: Tests to be carried out by a laboratory/ facility accredited by UKAS (United Kingdom

2. Standards::   

2.1. To BS 4551 - Mortars and screeds.

3. Apparatus:: Maintain and retain on site all necessary thermometers to ensure accurate readings.
4. Methodology::   

4.1. Samples for testing to be taken from the mixing point.
4.2. Repeat tests until performance requirements are satisfied.
4.3. Prepare test samples/ specimens at least 42 days prior to planned use in the Works.
4.4. Replace and re test any walling erected using mortar which fails a test.

510 Workmanship 
1. Storage: Store materials in a dry location, raised above ground and under cover to protect from 

rain, damp, frost, etc.
2. Equipment: Keep all tools and equipment clean and in good working order.
3. Techniques: Fully trained operatives to employ tried and tested techniques to achieve a workable, 

lump free and appropriate/ consistent mix. Mix by suitable mechanical means using dry sand and 
mortar containing air-entraining admixtures where appropriate or recommended by the 
manufacturer.

4. Weather conditions: Mortar temperature to be a minimum of 4℃ at all times. Replace mortar which 
has been affected by frost or temperatures below 4℃.

5. Standards:   
5.1. Butter masonry with mortar prior to laying.
5.2. Each course to be filled.
5.3. Maintain a consistent thickness of all joints.
5.4. Remove excess mortar as the work proceeds and before it hardens.
5.5. Brush and wash off smears with soft brush and clean water.
5.6. Avoid staining caused by excessive washing or scrubbing.
5.7. Do not use cleaning or acid based products to clean.

310 Joints 
1. Description: As shown on the Working Drawings.
2. Primer, backing strip, bond breaker: Types recommended by sealant manufacturer.

Execution
610 Suitability of joints 
1. Presealing checks   

1.1. Joint dimensions: Within limits specified for the sealant.
1.2. Substrate quality: Surfaces regular, undamaged and stable.

2. Primer: Joints to be carefully primed, without spillage or seeping, to achieve a full bond, without 
causing damage or change in appearance. Use masking tape where necessary.

620 Preparing joints 
1. Surfaces to which sealant must adhere   

1.1. Remove temporary coatings, tapes, loosely adhering material, dust, oil, grease, surface 
water and contaminants that may affect bond.

1.2. Clean using materials and methods recommended by sealant manufacturer.
1.3. Non porous surfaces such as metal, to be cleaned with an appropriate chemical agent, 

which does not damage the substrate or sealant in any way.
2. Vulnerable surfaces adjacent to joints: Mask to prevent staining or smearing with primer or sealant.
3. Backing strip and/ or bond breaker installation: Insert into joint to correct depth, without stretching 

or twisting, leaving no gaps.
4. Protection: Keep joints clean and protect from damage until sealant is applied.

630 Applying sealants 
1. Substrate: Dry (unless recommended otherwise) and unaffected by frost, ice or snow.
2. Trade recommendations: In accordance with the “Industry Guide to the Professional Application of 

Construction Sealants on Site” published by  BASA (British Adhesives and Sealants Association).
3. Environmental conditions: Do not dry or raise temperature of joints by heating.
4. Sealant application: Fill joints evenly, completely and neatly, ensuring firm adhesion to substrates, 

without bubbling using appropriate tools.
5. Sealant profiles   

5.1. Butt and lap joints: Slightly concave.
5.2. Fillet joints: Flat or slightly convex.

6. Joint fillers:   
6.1. To be entirely compatible and suitable.
6.2. Gaps in joints to be consistent with depth of sealant.
6.3. Sealant cross section in joint to be a ratio of 2:1 - width to depth - unless agreed otherwise.
6.4. Generate fillers from closed cell foam.
6.5. Fillers are not to adhere to cured sealant - use bond breaker tape if necessary.

7. Protection: Protect finished joints from contamination or damage until sealant has cured.

2.3. Reference number.
2.4. Name.
2.5. Gloss level.

235 Independent inspection at plant 
1. Requirement: Contractors/ suppliers of the following designated components must commission an 

Approved Independent Inspection Authority to carry out acceptance inspections to confirm that 
powder coating application complies with the Specification. 

2. Acceptance inspections: Carry out for each variation of colour and finish of each component work 
package at applicator's plant prior to any fabrication of units, in accordance with the following: 
2.1. Where three of more production runs are required for application of coatings, not less than 

three acceptance inspections must be carried out in accordance with BS 6001-1, general 
inspection level 2, with an acceptance quality limit of 1%.

2.2. Where less than three production runs are required for application of coatings, one 
acceptance inspection must be carried out in accordance with BS 6001-2, with a limiting 
quality of 5% where the probability of acceptance is 10%.

3. Components failing inspection: Reprocess or replace and reinspect.
4. Inspection reports: Independent Inspection Authority must submit copies.

tested and approved in accordance with the Qualicoat system.
2. Standard:   

2.1. Qualicoat Class 1 - Standard durability.
2.2. Qualicoat Class 2 - Super durability.

310 Pretreatment of aluminium components 
1. Condition of components to be pretreated   

1.1. Free from corrosion and damage.
1.2. All welding and jointing completed and finish off as specified.
1.3. Free from impurities including soil, grease and oil.
1.4. Suitable for and compatible with the pretreatment process.

2. Conversion coating requirements   
2.1. Chromate system: To BS 6496 or BS EN 12206-1.
2.2. Chromate-free system: To BS EN 12206-1. Submit details before using.

3. Rinsing requirements: Use demineralized water. Drain and dry.

320 Pretreatment of steel components 
1. Condition of components to be pretreated   

1.1. Free from corrosion and damage.
1.2. All welding and jointing completed and finish off as specified.
1.3. Free from impurities including soil, grease and oil.
1.4. Suitable for and compatible with the pretreatment process.

2. Conversion coating requirements: To BS EN 13438.
3. Rinsing requirements: Use demineralized water. Drain and dry.

330 Pretreatment for protection in aggressive environments 
1. Minimum thickness of 60 microns across significant and/ or primary surfaces.
2. Minimum thickness of 25 microns on non-significant and/ or secondary faces ensuring a coherent 

film layer.
3. All cut edges, drilled holes and mitres to be fully sealed.
4. Cleaning and maintenance: Carried out once every three to twelve months (dependent on 

proximity to pollutant).

430 Extent of powder coatings 
1. Application: To visible component surfaces, and concealed surfaces requiring protection. Coated 

surfaces will be deemed 'significant surfaces' for relevant BS 6496 or BS EN 13438 performance 
requirements.

435 Application of powder coatings 
1. Surfaces to receive powder coatings: Free from dust or powder deposits.
2. Powder colours: Obtain from one batch of one manufacturer.
3. Commencement of powder coating: To be continuous from pretreatment.
4. Components to be installed on site in order of application.
5. Jig points: Not visible on coated components.
6. Curing: Controlled to attain metal temperatures and hold periods recommended by powder coating 

manufacturer.

Anodizing

105 Standards 
1. Comply with: 

1.1. BS 3987 - Anodic oxidation.
1.2. BS 6161-18 - Abrasion resistance.
1.3. BS 6110-1 - Level 2 general inspection.
1.4. BS 6001-2 - Limited Quality (Pa=10%)+5% - damaged elements with no test results. To be 

assessed as individual components.
1.5. BS EN 1463 - Film thickness (range 25 to 35 microns).
1.6. BS EN ISO 2360 - Coating thickness for non conductive coatings on non magnetic 

electrically conductive base metal using amplitude sensitive Eddy Current method.
1.7. BS EN ISO 2931 - Sealing.
1.8. BS EN ISO 3210 - Maintaining weight loss below 30mg/d￿.
1.9. BS EN ISO 7559.
1.10. BS EN ISO 8251 - Testing - Wear index <1.4.

110 Anodic coating 
1. Selected anodizer: Submit details before commencement of anodizing, including: 

1.1. Name and contact details.
1.2. Details of accreditation schemes.
1.3. Technical data of product including current Agrément certificates.

2. Process: 
2.1. Two stage electrolytic using cobalt or nickel colouring agent. (Note - single stage integral 

process may be used with prior authorisation).
2.2. Do not use tin electrolytes.
2.3. Consistency of finish and colour to be the same throughout.
2.4. Alloy extrusions to be Grade AW-6063.
2.5. Alloy sheets to be Grade J57S.
2.6. Provide all necessary certification to demonstrate compliance and suitability.
2.7. Source from single manufacturer and single batch.

210 Working procedures 
1. Standard: To BS 3987 for anodic coatings on wrought aluminium.

220 Anodizer requirements 
1. Processing   

1.1. Approved: By the Aluminium Finishing Association.
1.2. Certified: To BS EN ISO 9001.
1.3. Anodizing plant: Each anodizer to use only one plant.

255 Quality assurance system 
1. Requirement:   

1.1. Powder and coating application to components is to be tested and accepted in accordance 
with the Qualanod system. 

1.2. Commence coating after all machining and fabrication is complete.
1.3. All visible elements, including edges to be coated.
1.4. Uncoated edges, etc. to be non visible in the completed installation.
1.5. Surfaces to have high quality, consistent and uniform appearance, including colour and 

texture.
1.6. Apply appropriate sealants as required.
1.7. Ensure that anodised finishes are weather resistant, compatible, resistant to abrasion/ 

impact/ chemical attack.
Surface blemishes will not be accepted if visible from 1000mm.

1.8. Ensure that anodised finishes are weather resistant, compatible, resistant to abrasion/ 
impact/ chemical attack.
Impregnated cold sealing is not acceptable.

1.9. Ensure that anodised finishes are weather resistant, compatible, resistant to abrasion/ 
impact/ chemical attack.
Use a sulphuric acid bath process to achieve high quality anodic oxidation.

1.10. Regularly clean extrusion dies.
